Homotopy theory of post-Lie algebras
Andrey Lazarev, Yunhe Sheng, Rong Tang

Abstract
. In this paper, we study the homotopy theory of post-Lie algebras. Guided by Koszul duality theory, we consider the graded Lie algebra of coderivations of the cofree conilpotent graded cocommutative cotrialgebra generated by V V . We show that in the case of V V being a shift of an ungraded vector space W W , Maurer-Cartan elements of this graded Lie algebra are exactly post-Lie algebra structures on W W . The cohomology of a post-Lie algebra is then defined using Maurer-Cartan twisting. The second cohomology group of a post-Lie algebra has a familiar interpretation as equivalence classes of infinitesimal deformations. Next we define a post-Lie ∞ algebra structure on a graded vector space to be a Maurer-Cartan element of the aforementioned graded Lie algebra. Post-Lie ∞ algebras admit a useful characterization in terms of L ∞ L_{\infty} -actions (or open-closed homotopy Lie algebras). Finally, we introduce the notion of homotopy Rota-Baxter operators on open-closed homotopy Lie algebras and show that certain homotopy Rota-Baxter operators induce post-Lie ∞ algebras.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Homotopy theories

Homotopy invariant algebraic structures play a prominent role in modern mathematical physics
[
49
]
.
Historically, the notion of
A_{\infty}
-algebras, which was introduced by Stasheff
in his study of based loop spaces
[
48
]
, was the first such structure.
Relevant later developments include the work of Lada and Stasheff about

L_{\infty}
-algebras in mathematical physics
[
29
]

and the work of Chapoton and Livernet about
pre-Lie
∞
algebras
[
14
]
. Strong homotopy (or infinity-) versions of a large class of algebraic
structures were studied in the context of
operads in
[
33
,
36
,
41
,
42
]
. A strong homotopy algebra is typically a Maurer-Cartan element in a certain differential graded (dg) Lie algebra (or possibly an
L_{\infty}
-algebra) and its Maurer-Cartan twisting is called the cohomology of said strong homotopy algebra; it is known to control its deformation theory. Another important notion, particularly relevant to the present paper, is that of an
L_{\infty}
-action or, more generally, an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra introduced in
[
15
,
37
,
40
]
.

1.2.
Post-Lie algebras

The notion of a post-Lie algebra has been introduced by Vallette in the course of study of Koszul duality of operads
[
51
]
.
Munthe-Kaas and his coauthors found that post-Lie algebras also naturally appear in differential geometry and numerical integration on manifolds
[
44
]
. Meanwhile, it was found that post-Lie algebras play an essential role in regularity structures in stochastic analysis
[
9
,
10
,
25
]
.
Recently, post-Lie algebras have been studied from different points of view including constructions of nonabelian generalized Lax pairs
[
6
]
, Poincaré-Birkhoff-Witt type theorems
[
18
,
23
]
, factorization theorems
[
20
]
and relations to post-Lie groups
[
2
,
4
,
38
]
.

A pre-Lie algebra is a post-Lie algebra whose underlying Lie algebra is abelian; this notion is important in its own right
[
11
,
34
,
47
]
. The homotopy theory of pre-Lie algebras was given in
[
14
]
using the operadic approach. Furthermore, the cohomology theory of pre-Lie algebras was given by Dzhumadil’daev and Boyom in
[
19
]
and
[
8
]
.
It is, therefore, natural to develop the homotopy theory for post-Lie algebras, its cohomology theory and a strong homotopy version of a post-Lie algebra. The first step in this direction was
[
50
]
, where the notion of operator homotopy post-Lie algebras was introduced. A cohomology theory corresponding to operator homotopy post-Lie algebras was also given in
[
50
]
. In this paper, the homotopy theory of post-Lie algebras is developed in full generality, including the notion of post-Lie
∞
algebra and the concomitant cohomology theory.

1.3.
Rota-Baxter operators

The notion of Rota-Baxter operators on associative algebras was
introduced by G. Baxter in his study of
fluctuation theory in probability
[
7
]
. Rota-Baxter operators play important roles in Connes-Kreimer’s
[
16
]
algebraic
approach to the renormalization in perturbative quantum field
theory, the splitting of
operads
[
1
,
5
]
and double Poisson algebras
[
21
,
22
]
.
In the Lie algebra context, a Rota-Baxter operator was introduced as the
operator form of the classical Yang-Baxter equation. To better understand the classical Yang-Baxter equation and
related integrable systems, the more general notion of an
{\mathcal{O}}
-operator (also called relative Rota-Baxter operator)
on a Lie algebra was introduced by Kupershmidt
[
28
]
. Relative Rota-Baxter operators naturally give rise to pre-Lie algebras or post-Lie algebras
[
3
,
6
]
. For further details on
Rota-Baxter operators, see
[
24
]
.

The homotopy of Rota-Baxter associative algebras were studied in
[
17
]
, where it was noted that “in general compact formulas
are yet to be found”. Recently the homotopy theory of Rota-Baxter associative algebras was given in
[
54
]
. For Rota-Baxter Lie algebras, one encounters a similarly challenging situation. Using higher derived brackets and Maurer-Cartan elements approach, the notion of a homotopy relative Rota-Baxter operator on an
L_{\infty}
-algebra with respect to a representation was formulated in
[
32
]
. Remarkably, strict homotopy relative Rota-Baxter operators give rise to pre-Lie
∞
algebras. Later, homotopy relative Rota-Baxter operators on an
L_{\infty}
-algebra with respect to an
L_{\infty}
-action were studied in
[
12
]
. However, the underlying algebraic structures were still yet to be found. In this paper we introduce, on the one hand, the notion of homotopy Rota-Baxter operators on an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra using higher derived brackets; this contains the constructions given in
[
12
]
as special cases. On the other hand, we show that post-Lie
∞
algebras are the underlying algebraic structures of certain homotopy Rota-Baxter operators.

1.4.
Main results and outline of the paper

Since the Koszul dual of the operad
\mathsf{PostLie}
for post-Lie algebras is the operad
\mathsf{ComTrias}
whose algebras are commutative trialgebras
[
51
]
, we are led to study graded Lie algebra of coderivations of the cofree conilpotent graded cocommutative cotrialgebra generated by a graded vector space
V
. If
V
is a shift of an ordinary vector space
W
, Maurer-Cartan elements of this graded Lie algebra are exactly post-Lie algebra structures on
W
. Consequently, we obtain the dg Lie algebra that governs deformations of post-Lie algebras; the differential in it is the commutator with the given Maurer-Cartan element. This is the content of Section
2
.

In Section
3
, we define the cohomology of a post-Lie algebra using the differential in the aforementioned dg Lie algebra that governs deformations of post-Lie algebras. We give a precise description of the coboundary operator, which can be viewed as a combination of the Chevalley-Eilenberg coboundary operator for a Lie algebra and the coboundary operator for a pre-Lie algebra. As an application, we classify infinitesimal deformations of a post-Lie algebra in terms the second cohomology group of the constructed complex.

In Section
4
, we define post-Lie
∞
algebra structures as Maurer-Cartan elements of the aforementioned graded Lie algebra associated to a graded vector space. A post-Lie
∞
algebra give rise to a sub-adjacent
L_{\infty}
-algebra which is itself part of a certain open-closed homotopy Lie algebra. We show how the original post-Lie
∞
algebra is characterized in terms of this associated open-closed homotopy Lie algebra. Higher geometric structures, such as homotopy Poisson algebras and
L_{\infty}
-algebroids give rise to post-Lie
∞
algebras naturally, providing a wealth of examples of these structures.

In Section
5
, we introduce the notion of homotopy Rota-Baxter operators on an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra; these are certain Maurer-Cartan elements on
L_{\infty}
-algebras given by the higher derived brackets construction. Since actions of
L_{\infty}
-algebras are special open-closed homotopy Lie algebras, so homotopy Rota-Baxter operators on an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra contain
{\mathcal{O}}
-operators on an
L_{\infty}
-algebra with respect to an action as special cases. In the classical case, a Rota-Baxter operator induces a post-Lie algebra. Now in the homotopy setting, we also show that certain homotopy Rota-Baxter operators induces post-Lie
∞
algebras.

1.5.
Notation and conventions

We work in the category of
cohomologically
\mathbb{Z}
-graded dg vector spaces
\operatorname{DGVect_{{\mathbf{k}}}}
(equivalently, cochain complexes) over a ground field

{\mathbf{k}}
of characteristic zero.
Symbols
\otimes
and
\mathrm{Hom}
stand for tensor products and homomorphisms over
{\mathbf{k}}
. A dg associative algebra is a dg vector space
A
together with an associative and unital product
m:A\otimes A\to A
; formally an associative monoid in
\operatorname{DGVect_{{\mathbf{k}}}}
. We will also need the notions of a commutative dg algebra (a commutative and associative monoid in
\operatorname{DGVect_{{\mathbf{k}}}}
) and also that of a dg Lie algebra, which is a Lie object in
\operatorname{DGVect}
. Similarly, a (cocommutative) dg coalgebra is a comonoid in
\operatorname{DGVect_{{\mathbf{k}}}}
. Thus, a dg coalgebra
C
has a comultiplication
\Delta:C\to C\otimes C
. The
n
-fold iteration of
\Delta
is the map
\Delta^{n}={(\Delta\otimes\rm{Id}\otimes\ldots\otimes\rm{Id})\circ\ldots\circ\Delta}:C\to C^{\otimes(n+1)}
and we say that
C
is
conilpotent
if for any
c\in C
there exists
N\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\Delta^{N}(c)=0
.

Given a dg vector space
V
, we will denote by
\mathsf{T}(V)
its tensor algebra and by
\mathsf{S}(V)
its symmetric algebra which is the quotient of
\mathsf{T}(V)
by the
2
-sided ideal of
\mathsf{T}(V)
generated by all homogeneous elements
x,y\in V
of the form

x\otimes y-(-1)^{xy}y\otimes x.

We will write
\mathbb{S}_{n}
for the group of permutations on
n
symbols. A permutation
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{n}
is called an
(i,n-i)
-shuffle if
\sigma(1)<\ldots<\sigma(i)
and
\sigma(i+1)<\ldots<\sigma(n)
. If
i=0
or
n
, we assume
\sigma=\rm{Id}
. The set of all
(i,n-i)
-shuffles will be denoted by
\mathbb{S}_{(i,n-i)}
. The notion of an
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k})
-shuffle and the set
\mathbb{S}_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k})}
are defined analogously.

Denote the product of homogeneous elements
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\in V
in
\mathsf{S}^{n}(V)
by
v_{1}\ldots v_{n}
. The degree of
v_{1}\ldots v_{n}
is by definition the sum of the degree of
v_{i}
. For a permutation
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{n}
and
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\in V
, the Koszul sign
\varepsilon(\sigma;v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})\in\{-1,1\}
is defined by

\displaystyle v_{1}\ldots v_{n}=\varepsilon(\sigma;v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})v_{\sigma(1)}\ldots v_{\sigma(n)}.

Moreover, there is a coalgebra structure on
\mathsf{S}(V)
given by
\Delta(1_{\mathbf{k}})=1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes 1_{\mathbf{k}}
and

\displaystyle\Delta(v_{1}\ldots v_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{n}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,n-i)}}\varepsilon(\sigma;v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})\big(v_{\sigma(1)}\ldots v_{\sigma(i)}\big)\otimes\big(v_{\sigma(i+1)}\ldots v_{\sigma(n)}\big).

The counit
\varepsilon:\mathsf{S}(V)\,\rightarrow\,{\mathbf{k}}
is defined by

\varepsilon(1_{\mathbf{k}})=1_{\mathbf{k}}
and
\varepsilon(v_{1}\ldots v_{n})=0.

Next, the coaugmentation map
\mu:{\mathbf{k}}\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{S}(V)
is defined by

\mu(1_{\mathbf{k}})=1_{\mathbf{k}}.

Then
(\mathsf{S}(V),\Delta,\varepsilon,\mu)
is the cofree conilpotent cocommutative coalgebra. More precisely, for any conilpotent cocommutative coalgebra
(C,\Delta_{C},\varepsilon_{C},\mu_{C})
and any linear map
f:C\,\rightarrow\,V
satisfying
f(1_{C})=0
, there exists a unique coalgebra homomorphism
\bar{f}:C\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{S}(V)
such that
\mathrm{pr}_{V}\circ\bar{f}=f
,
where
\bar{f}
is given by

(1)

\displaystyle\bar{f}(1_{C})=1_{\mathbf{k}},\,\,\bar{f}(x)=\sum_{i=1}^{+\infty}\sum_{x}\frac{1}{i!}f(x_{(1)})\ldots f(x_{(i)}),\,\,\bar{\Delta}_{C}^{i-1}(x)=\sum_{x}x_{(1)}\otimes\ldots\otimes x_{(i)},

for all
x\in\bar{C}=\ker\varepsilon_{C}
. Here
\bar{\Delta}_{C}
is the reduced coproduct
\bar{\Delta}_{C}:\bar{C}\,\rightarrow\,\bar{C}\otimes\bar{C}
given by

\displaystyle\bar{\Delta}_{C}(x)=\Delta_{C}(x)-x\otimes 1_{C}-1_{C}\otimes x,\,\,\,\,\forall x\in\bar{C}.

2.
The controlling algebra of post-Lie algebras

In this section, we associate to any graded vector space
V
the graded Lie algebra of coderivations of the cofree conilpotent graded cocommutative cotrialgebra generated by
V
. We show that in the case of
V
being a shift of an ungraded vector space
W
, this graded Lie algebra is exactly the controlling algebra of post-Lie algebras, i.e. its Maurer-Cartan elements are post-Lie algebra structures on
W
.

Definition 2.1
.

(
[
51
]
)
A
post-Lie algebra

(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd)
consists of a Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}})
and a binary product
\rhd:\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{g}
such that

(2)

\displaystyle x\rhd[y,z]_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[x\rhd y,z]_{\mathfrak{g}}+[y,x\rhd z]_{\mathfrak{g}},

(3)

\displaystyle([x,y]_{\mathfrak{g}}+x\rhd y-y\rhd x)\rhd z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x\rhd(y\rhd z)-y\rhd(x\rhd z),\,\,\forall x,y,z\in\mathfrak{g}.

Any post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd)
gives rise to a new Lie algebra

\mathfrak{g}_{\rhd}:=(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rhd}})

defined by

[x,y]_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rhd}}\coloneqq x\rhd y-y\rhd x+[x,y]_{\mathfrak{g}},\quad\forall x,y\in\mathfrak{g},

which is called the
sub-adjacent Lie algebra
. Moreover,
Eqs. (
2
)-(
3
) equivalently mean that the linear map
L:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{g})
defined by
L_{x}y=x\rhd y
is an action of
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rhd}})
on
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}})
.

Remark 2.2
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd)
be a post-Lie algebra. If the Lie bracket
[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}}
vanishes, then
(\mathfrak{g},\rhd)
becomes a pre-Lie algebra. Thus, a post-Lie algebra can be viewed as a nonabelian generalization of a pre-Lie algebra.

Definition 2.3
.

•

A
derivation
of a post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd)
is a linear map
\varphi:\mathfrak{g}\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{g}
satisfying
\varphi([x,y]_{\mathfrak{g}})=[\varphi(x),y]_{\mathfrak{g}}+[x,\varphi(y)]_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\varphi(x\rhd y)=\varphi(x)\rhd y+x\rhd\varphi(y)
for all
x,y\in\mathfrak{g}.

Denote by
\mathsf{Der}(\mathfrak{g})
the set of derivations of the post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd)
.

•

A
homomorphism

\phi
from a post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
to a post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{h},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{h}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{h}})
is a Lie algebra morphism
\phi:\mathfrak{g}\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{h}
such that

\phi(x\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}y)=\phi(x)\rhd_{\mathfrak{h}}\phi(y)
for all
x,y\in\mathfrak{g}.

In particular, if
\phi
is invertible, then
\phi
is called an
isomorphism
.

Example 2.4
.

Let
(V,\rhd)
be a magmatic algebra, i.e.
V
is a vector space and
\rhd:V\otimes V\to V
is an arbitrary linear operation,
[
33
, Definition 13.8.1]
. Extend the magmatic operation
\rhd:V\otimes V\to V

to the free Lie algebra
(Lie(V),[\cdot,\cdot])
. Then
(Lie(V),[\cdot,\cdot],\rhd)
is a post-Lie algebra.

Example 2.5

(Free post-Lie algebra)
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}
be the set of isomorphism classes of planar rooted trees:

Let
{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{O}}\}
be the free
{\mathbf{k}}
-vector space generated by
{\mathcal{O}}
. The
left grafting operator

\rhd:{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{O}}\}\otimes{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{O}}\}\to{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{O}}\}
is defined by

(4)

\displaystyle\tau\rhd\omega=\sum_{s\in{\rm Nodes}(\omega)}\tau\circ_{s}\omega,\,\,\forall\tau,\omega\in{\mathcal{O}},\vskip-2.84544pt

where
\tau\circ_{s}\omega
is the planar rooted tree resulting from attaching the root of
\tau
to the node
s
of the tree
\omega
from the left. Consider the free Lie algebra
Lie({\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{O}}\})
generated by the vector space
{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{O}}\}
and extend the left grafting operator
\rhd
on
{\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{O}}\}
to the free Lie algebra
Lie({\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{O}}\})
by (
2
) and (
3
). Then
(Lie({\mathbf{k}}\{{\mathcal{O}}\}),[\cdot,\cdot],\rhd)
is a post-Lie algebra, which is the free post-Lie algebra generated by one generator
\{\scalebox{0.6}{
\hbox to4.17pt{\vbox to4.17pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-2.08551pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.61 0 C 2.61 1.44 1.44 2.61 0 2.61 C -1.44 2.61 -2.61 1.44 -2.61 0 C -2.61 -1.44 -1.44 -2.61 0 -2.61 C 1.44 -2.61 2.61 -1.44 2.61 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.4}{0.0}{0.0}{0.4}{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(0.4 0.0 0.0 0.4 0 0)} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
}\}

[
44
,
51
]
.

Example 2.6

(Ihara Lie algebra)
.

Any element
f
of the free Lie algebra
Lie({\mathbf{k}}\{x,y\})
gives rise to a derivation
D_{f}
by

D_{f}(x)=0,\,\,\,\,D_{f}(y)=[y,f].

Moreover, for all
f,g\in Lie({\mathbf{k}}\{x,y\})
, define
\rhd:Lie({\mathbf{k}}\{x,y\})\otimes Lie({\mathbf{k}}\{x,y\})\,\rightarrow\,Lie({\mathbf{k}}\{x,y\})
by

\displaystyle f\rhd g=D_{f}(g),\,\,\,\,\forall f,g\in Lie({\mathbf{k}}\{x,y\}).

Then
(Lie({\mathbf{k}}\{x,y\}),[\cdot,\cdot],\rhd)
is a post-Lie algebra, whose sub-adjacent Lie algebra is exactly the Ihara Lie algebra
[
46
]
.

Post-Lie algebras are algebras over the operad
\mathsf{PostLie}
. Its Koszul dual is the operad
\mathsf{ComTrias}
whose algebras are commutative trialgebras,
[
51
]
. We will now recall the definition of a commutative trialgebra.

Definition 2.7
.

A
commutative trialgebra
is a triple
(A,\cdot,\ast)
, where
(A,\cdot)
is a commutative associative algebra and

(A,\ast)
is a permutative algebra, such that the following compatibility relations hold:

(5)

\displaystyle(x\cdot y)\ast z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(x\ast y)\ast z

(6)

\displaystyle x\ast(y\cdot z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(x\ast y)\cdot z.

Example 2.8
.

(
[
51
, Theorem 28]
) Let
V
be a graded vector space. There is a commutative trialgebra structure on
\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V)
given by

(7)

\displaystyle\big(x_{1}\ldots x_{m}\otimes y_{1}\ldots y_{n}\big)\cdot\big(u_{1}\ldots u_{k}\otimes v_{1}\ldots v_{l}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\alpha}x_{1}\ldots x_{m}u_{1}\ldots u_{k}\otimes y_{1}\ldots y_{n}v_{1}\ldots v_{l},

(8)

\displaystyle\quad\big(x_{1}\ldots x_{m}\otimes y_{1}\ldots y_{n}\big)\ast\big(u_{1}\ldots u_{k}\otimes v_{1}\ldots v_{l}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x_{1}\ldots x_{m}y_{1}\ldots y_{n}u_{1}\ldots u_{k}\otimes v_{1}\ldots v_{l},

where
\alpha=(y_{1}+\ldots+y_{n})(u_{1}+\ldots+u_{k})
.
Moreover,
(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),\cdot,\ast)
is the free graded commutative trialgebra generated by the graded vector space
V
and we denote it by
\mathsf{ComTrias}(V)
.

Definition 2.9
.

A
derivation
of a graded commutative trialgebra
(A,\cdot,\ast)
is a graded linear map
d:A\,\rightarrow\,A
such that for all
x,y\in A,
the following equalities hold:

(9)

\displaystyle d(x\cdot y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d(x)\cdot y+(-1)^{xd}x\cdot d(y),

(10)

\displaystyle d(x\ast y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d(x)\ast y+(-1)^{xd}x\ast d(y).

Let
d\in\mathrm{Hom}(V,\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V))
. Define
\Phi(d):\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V)\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V)
by

(11)

\displaystyle\Phi(d)(x_{1}\ldots x_{m}\otimes y_{1}\ldots y_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}(-1)^{(x_{1}+\ldots+x_{i-1})d}\big(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{1}\ast\ldots\ast d(x_{i})\ast\ldots\ast 1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{m}\big)\ast\big(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot 1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{n}\big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}(-1)^{(x_{1}+\ldots+x_{m}+y_{1}+\ldots+y_{j-1})d}\big(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{1}\ast\ldots\ast 1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{m}\big)\ast\big(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot d(y_{j})\cdot\ldots\cdot 1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{n}\big)

for all
x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}\in V.

Proposition 2.10
.

With notations as above,
\Phi
is an isomorphism from the graded vector space
\mathrm{Hom}(V,\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V))
to
\mathsf{Der}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V))
.

Proof.

We define a graded linear map
\mathfrak{i}:\mathsf{Der}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V))\,\rightarrow\,\mathrm{Hom}(V,\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V))
as follows:

(12)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{i}(f)=f\circ i_{V},\,\,\,\,\forall f\in\mathsf{Der}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V)).

Here
i_{V}:V\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V))
is the embedding given by

i_{V}(v)=1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes v
for all
v\in V.

For any
d\in\mathrm{Hom}(V,\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V))
, by (
11
) and (
12
), we have

\displaystyle\big((\mathfrak{i}\circ\Phi)(d)\big)(v)=\Phi(d)(i_{V}(v))=\Phi(d)(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes v)=d(v).

Therefore
\mathfrak{i}\circ\Phi=\rm{Id}
. On the other hand, for any
f\in\mathsf{Der}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V))
, we obtain that

\displaystyle f(x_{1}\ldots x_{m}\otimes y_{1}\ldots y_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f\big((1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{1}\ast\ldots\ast 1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{m})\ast(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot 1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{n})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f\big((1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{1}\ast\ldots\ast 1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{m})\big)\ast(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot 1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{n})

\displaystyle+(-1)^{(x_{1}+\ldots+x_{m})d}(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{1}\ast\ldots\ast 1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{m})\ast f\big((1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot 1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{n})\big)

\displaystyle{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}(-1)^{(x_{1}+\ldots+x_{i-1})d}\big(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{1}\ast\ldots\ast f(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{i})\ast\ldots\ast 1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{m}\big)\ast\big(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot 1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{n}\big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}(-1)^{(x_{1}+\ldots+x_{m}+y_{1}+\ldots+y_{j-1})d}\big(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{1}\ast\ldots\ast 1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes x_{m}\big)\ast\big(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot f(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{j})\cdot\ldots\cdot 1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes y_{n}\big).

Moreover, (
11
) implies that
f=\Phi(\mathfrak{i}(f))
, i.e.
\Phi\circ\mathfrak{i}=\rm{Id}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Definition 2.11
.

A
cocommutative cotrialgebra
is a triple
(C,\Delta,\delta)
, where
(C,\Delta)
is a cocommutative coassociative coalgebra:

(13)

\displaystyle\Delta=\tau\circ\Delta,\,\,\,\,(\rm{Id}\otimes\Delta)\circ\Delta=(\Delta\otimes\rm{Id})\circ\Delta,

(C,\delta)
is a copermutative coalgebra:

(14)

\displaystyle(\rm{Id}\otimes\delta)\circ\delta=(\delta\otimes\rm{Id})\circ\delta=(\tau\otimes\rm{Id})\circ(\delta\otimes\rm{Id})\circ\delta,

and the following compatibility relations hold:

(15)

\displaystyle(\Delta\otimes\rm{Id})\circ\delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\delta\otimes\rm{Id})\circ\delta,

(16)

\displaystyle(\delta\otimes\rm{Id})\circ\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\rm{Id}\otimes\Delta)\circ\delta,

where
\tau(a\otimes b)=(-1)^{ab}b\otimes a
.

A cocommutative cotrialgebra
(C,\Delta,\delta)
is called conilpotent if for any
x\in C
there exists
N\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\theta^{n}(x)=0
for
n>N
, where
\theta^{n}:C\to C^{\otimes(n+1)}
is any
n
-fold iteration of
\Delta
or
\delta
.

Theorem 2.12
.

Let
V
be a graded vector space. Then there is a cocommutative cotrialgebra structure on
\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V)
given by

\displaystyle\Delta(x_{1}\ldots x_{m}\otimes y_{1}\ldots y_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq i\leq m\atop 1\leq j\leq n-1}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,m-i)}\atop\tau\in\mathbb{S}_{(j,n-j)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)\varepsilon(\tau)(-1)^{(x_{\sigma(i+1)}+\ldots+x_{\sigma(m)})(y_{\tau(1)}+\ldots+y_{\tau(j)})}

\displaystyle\big(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(i)}\otimes y_{\tau(1)}\ldots y_{\tau(j)}\big)\otimes\big(x_{\sigma(i+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(m)}\otimes y_{\tau(j+1)}\ldots y_{\tau(n)}\big),

\displaystyle\delta(x_{1}\ldots x_{m}\otimes y_{1}\ldots y_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq i\leq m-1\atop 1\leq j\leq m}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,j,m-i-j)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)

\displaystyle\big(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(i)}\otimes x_{\sigma(i+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(i+j)}\big)\otimes\big(x_{\sigma(i+j+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(m)}\otimes y_{1}\ldots y_{n}\big).

Moreover,
(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),\Delta,\delta)
is the cofree conilpotent graded cocommutative cotrialgebra generated by the graded vector space
V
and we denote it by
\mathsf{ComTrias}^{c}(V)
.

Proof.

Since
(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),\Delta,\delta)
is the graded dual of the free graded commutative trialgebra
(\mathsf{S}(V^{*})\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V^{*}),\cdot,\ast)
, it follows that
(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),\Delta,\delta)
is a graded cocommutative cotrialgebra. It is clear that it is conilpotent. Let us show that it is cofree. For
m,n\geq 1
, we write

\Delta^{n}=(\Delta\otimes{\rm{Id}}^{\otimes n-1})\ldots(\Delta\otimes{\rm{Id}})\Delta,\quad\delta^{m}=(\delta\otimes{{\rm{Id}}}^{\otimes m-1})\ldots(\delta\otimes\rm{Id})\delta.

Then we have

(17)

\displaystyle\ast(\ast_{m}\otimes\cdot_{n})((\delta^{m-1}\otimes\Delta^{n-1})\circ\delta)(x_{1}\ldots x_{m}\otimes y_{1}\ldots y_{n})=m!n!x_{1}\ldots x_{m}\otimes y_{1}\ldots y_{n},

where

\displaystyle\ast_{m}(X_{1}\otimes Y_{1},\ldots,X_{m}\otimes Y_{m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle X_{1}Y_{1}\ldots Y_{m-1}X_{m}\otimes Y_{m},

\displaystyle\cdot_{n}(X_{1}\otimes Y_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\otimes Y_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(Y_{1}+\ldots+Y_{i-1})X_{i}}X_{1}\ldots X_{n}\otimes Y_{1}\ldots Y_{n}.

Let
(C,\Delta,\delta)
be a graded conilpotent cocommutative cotrialgebra and
f:C\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V)
be a coalgebra homomorphism. Note that
f\in\mathrm{Hom}^{0}(C,\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V))
is the sum of
f^{m,n}:C\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{S}^{m}(V)\otimes\mathsf{S}^{n}(V),~\forall m\geq 0,n\geq 1
. Moreover, denote the projection
\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V)\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{S}^{m}(V)\otimes\mathsf{S}^{n}(V)
by
\mathrm{pr}^{m,n}_{V}
. Then
f^{0,1}=\mathrm{pr}^{0,1}_{V}\circ f
. Since
f
is a coalgebra homomorphism, we have the following diagram:

(18)

\begin{split}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 58.30923pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.93124pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.78223pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 91.13742pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-58.30923pt\raise-16.11555pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.2128pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{$(\delta^{m-1}\otimes\Delta^{n-1})\circ\delta$}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.76886pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 43.39543pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 91.13742pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 120.24855pt\raise-16.11555pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.2128pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{$(\delta^{m-1}\otimes\Delta^{n-1})\circ\delta$}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 120.24855pt\raise-23.76886pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-19.39543pt\raise-32.23112pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\text{$C^{\otimes m+n}$}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.98232pt\raise-25.37498pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.49504pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f^{\otimes m+n}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.39543pt\raise-32.23112pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 43.39543pt\raise-32.23112pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 73.39543pt\raise-32.23112pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\text{$(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V))^{\otimes m+n}$}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

By (
17
) and (
18
), we obtain that

\mathrm{pr}^{m,n}_{V}\circ\big(\ast(\ast_{m}\otimes\cdot_{n})\big)\circ f^{\otimes m+n}\circ(\delta^{m-1}\otimes\Delta^{n-1})\circ\delta=m!n!f^{m,n}.

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle f^{m,n}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{x}\frac{1}{m!n!}\ast(\ast_{m}\otimes\cdot_{n})\Big(\big(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes f^{0,1}(x_{(1)})\big)\otimes\big(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes f^{0,1}(x_{(2)})\big)\otimes\ldots\otimes\big(1_{\mathbf{k}}\otimes f^{0,1}(x_{(m+n)})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{x}\frac{1}{m!n!}f^{0,1}(x_{(1)})\ldots f^{0,1}(x_{(m)})\otimes f^{0,1}(x_{(m+1)})\ldots f^{0,1}(x_{(m+n)}),

where
(\delta^{m-1}\otimes\Delta^{n-1})\delta(x)=\sum_{x}x_{(1)}\otimes x_{(2)}\otimes\ldots\otimes x_{(m+n)},
for all
x\in C.
Moreover, we can reconstruct the coalgebra homomorphism
f
by
f^{0,1}
as follows:

(19)

\displaystyle f(x)=\sum_{m\geq 0,n\geq 1}\sum_{x}\frac{1}{m!n!}f^{0,1}(x_{(1)})\ldots f^{0,1}(x_{(m)})\otimes f^{0,1}(x_{(m+1)})\ldots f^{0,1}(x_{(m+n)}).

Therefore, for any graded conilpotent cocommutative cotrialgebra
(C,\Delta,\delta)
and any linear map
\phi:C\,\rightarrow\,V
, by (
19
), there exists a unique coalgebra homomorphism
\Phi:C\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V)
such that we have the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}^{0,1}_{V}}
\textstyle{V}

Here
\Phi
is given by

(20)

\displaystyle\Phi(x)=\sum_{m\geq 0,n\geq 1}\sum_{x}\frac{1}{m!n!}\phi(x_{(1)})\ldots\phi(x_{(m)})\otimes\phi(x_{(m+1)})\ldots\phi(x_{(m+n)}).

This shows that
(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),\Delta,\delta)
is the cofree conilpotent cocommutative cotrialgebra generated by the graded vector space
V
.
∎

Definition 2.13
.

A
coderivation
of a graded cocommutative cotrialgebra
(C,\Delta,\delta)
is a graded linear map
D:C\,\rightarrow\,C
such that

(21)

\displaystyle\Delta\circ D

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(D\otimes{\rm{Id}}+{\rm{Id}}\otimes D)\circ\Delta,

(22)

\displaystyle\delta\circ D

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(D\otimes{\rm{Id}}+{\rm{Id}}\otimes D)\circ\delta.

Note that an element
\mathfrak{R}\in\mathrm{Hom}^{k}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),V)
is the sum of
\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}:\mathsf{S}^{p}(V)\otimes\mathsf{S}^{q}(V)\,\rightarrow\,V
. Write
\mathfrak{R}=\sum_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1}\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}
and

\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),V)=\oplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{Hom}^{n}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),V),

the decomposition of the space of homomorphisms into graded pieces.
For
\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}:\mathsf{S}^{p}(V)\otimes\mathsf{S}^{q}(V)\,\rightarrow\,V
, we define
\hat{\mathfrak{R}}_{p,q}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V))
by

(23)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{R}}_{p,q}(x_{1}\ldots x_{n}\otimes x_{n+1}\ldots x_{n+m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(p,q,n-p-q)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(p)}\otimes x_{\sigma(p+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(p+q)})x_{\sigma(p+q+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)}\otimes x_{n+1}\ldots x_{n+m}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(n-p,p)}}\sum_{\tau\in\mathbb{S}_{(q,m-q)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)\varepsilon(\tau)(-1)^{k(x_{\sigma(1)}+\ldots+x_{\sigma(n-p)})}

\displaystyle x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n-p)}\otimes\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}\big(x_{\sigma(n-p+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)}\otimes x_{n+\tau(1)}\ldots x_{n+\tau(q)}\big)x_{n+\tau(q+1)}\ldots x_{n+\tau(m)},

for all
n\geq p,m\geq q,
and

\hat{\mathfrak{R}}_{p,q}(x_{1}\ldots x_{n}\otimes x_{n+1}\ldots x_{m+n})=0
for other cases.

The following result holds.

Proposition 2.14
.

Define
\Psi:\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),V)\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{coDer}(\mathsf{ComTrias}^{c}(V))
by

\displaystyle\Psi(\mathfrak{R})(x_{1}\ldots x_{n}\otimes x_{n+1}\ldots x_{n+m})=\sum_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1}\hat{\mathfrak{R}}_{p,q}(x_{1}\ldots x_{n}\otimes x_{n+1}\ldots x_{n+m}).

Then
\Psi
is an isomorphism of graded vector spaces.

Proof.

Let
D\in\mathsf{coDer}(\mathsf{ComTrias}^{c}(V))
. Then
D
is the sum of
D^{m,n}:\mathsf{ComTrias}^{c}(V)\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{S}^{m}(V)\otimes\mathsf{S}^{n}(V),
for all
m\geq 0,n\geq 1
. Since
D
is a coderivation, we have the following commutative diagram:

(24)

\begin{split}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 58.30923pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-32.82858pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{ComTrias}^{c}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 70.49924pt\raise 5.39168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{D}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 120.92366pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-58.30923pt\raise-16.11555pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.2128pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{$(\delta^{m-1}\otimes\Delta^{n-1})\circ\delta$}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.76886pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 73.18167pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 120.92366pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{ComTrias}^{c}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 153.75224pt\raise-16.11555pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.2128pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{$(\delta^{m-1}\otimes\Delta^{n-1})\circ\delta$}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 153.75224pt\raise-23.76886pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-49.18167pt\raise-32.23112pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\text{$(\mathsf{ComTrias}^{c}(V))^{\otimes m+n}$}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.66891pt\raise-41.90675pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.32446pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sum_{i=1}^{m+n}{\rm{Id}}^{\otimes i-1}\otimes D\otimes{\rm{Id}}^{\otimes m+n-i}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 103.18167pt\raise-32.23112pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 73.18167pt\raise-32.23112pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 103.18167pt\raise-32.23112pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\text{$(\mathsf{ComTrias}^{c}(V))^{\otimes m+n}$}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

By (
17
) and (
24
), we obtain that

\mathrm{pr}^{m,n}_{V}\circ\big(\ast(\ast_{m}\otimes\cdot_{n})\big)\circ\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{m+n}{\rm{Id}}^{\otimes i-1}\otimes D\otimes{\rm{Id}}^{\otimes m+n-i}\Big)\circ(\delta^{m-1}\otimes\Delta^{n-1})\circ\delta=m!n!D^{m,n}.

Thus, we deduce that

\displaystyle D^{m,n}(X)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{X}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{m!n!}(-1)^{\alpha_{i}}\mathrm{pr}^{0,1}_{V}(X_{(1)})\ldots D^{0,1}(X_{(i)})\ldots\mathrm{pr}^{0,1}_{V}(X_{(m)})\otimes\mathrm{pr}^{0,1}_{V}(X_{(m+1)})\ldots\mathrm{pr}^{0,1}_{V}(X_{(m+n)})

\displaystyle+\sum_{X}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{m!n!}(-1)^{\beta_{j}}\mathrm{pr}^{0,1}_{V}(X_{(1)})\ldots\mathrm{pr}^{0,1}_{V}(X_{(m)})\otimes\mathrm{pr}^{0,1}_{V}(X_{(m+1)})\ldots D^{0,1}(X_{(m+j)})\ldots\mathrm{pr}^{0,1}_{V}(X_{(m+n)}),

where
\alpha_{i}=D(X_{(1)}+\ldots+X_{(i-1)})
,
\beta_{j}=D(X_{(1)}+\ldots+X_{(m+j-1)})
and
(\delta^{m-1}\otimes\Delta^{n-1})\delta(X)=\sum_{X}X_{(1)}\otimes X_{(2)}\otimes\ldots\otimes X_{(m+n)},
for all
X\in\mathsf{ComTrias}^{c}(V).
Therefore, the coderivation
D
is completely determined by
D^{0,1}
. Moreover, there holds
D=\Psi(D^{0,1})
. On the one hand, this shows that the map
\Psi
is well defined, and on the other hand, the map
\Psi
is surjective. It is obvious that the map
\Psi
is injective. Thus, the map
\Psi
is an isomorphism.
∎

Now we are ready to formulate the main result of this section.

Theorem 2.15
.

Let
V
be a graded vector space. Then
(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),V),[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{PL}})
is a graded Lie algebra, where the graded Lie bracket
[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{PL}}
is given by

\displaystyle[f,g]_{\mathsf{PL}}:=f\circ g-(-1)^{kl}g\circ f,\,\,\,\,

for all
f=\sum_{r\geq 0,s\geq 1}f_{r,s}\in\mathrm{Hom}^{k}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),V),g=\sum_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1}g_{p,q}\in\mathrm{Hom}^{l}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),V),

where
f\circ g\in\mathrm{Hom}^{k+l}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),V)
is defined by

\displaystyle(f\circ g)_{n,m}(x_{1}\ldots x_{n}\otimes x_{n+1}\ldots x_{n+m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(p,q,n-p-q)}\atop 0\leq p\leq n-1,1\leq q\leq n}\varepsilon(\sigma)f_{n-p-q+1,m}\Big(g_{p,q}(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(p)}\otimes x_{\sigma(p+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(p+q)})x_{\sigma(p+q+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)}\otimes x_{n+1}\ldots x_{n+m}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(n-p,p)}\atop 0\leq p\leq n}\sum_{\tau\in\mathbb{S}_{(q,m-q)}\atop 1\leq q\leq m}\varepsilon(\sigma)\varepsilon(\tau)(-1)^{l(x_{\sigma(1)}+\ldots+x_{\sigma(n-p)})}

\displaystyle f_{n-p,m-q+1}\Big(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n-p)}\otimes g_{p,q}(x_{\sigma(n-p+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)}\otimes x_{n+\tau(1)}\ldots x_{n+\tau(q)})x_{n+\tau(q+1)}\ldots x_{n+\tau(m)}\Big).

Proof.

It is obvious that
\mathsf{coDer}(\mathsf{ComTrias}^{c}(V))
is a graded Lie algebra, in which the graded Lie bracket
[\cdot,\cdot]_{C}
is just the commutator. Transferring the graded Lie algebra structure from
\big(\mathsf{coDer}(\mathsf{ComTrias}^{c}(V)),[\cdot,\cdot]_{C}\big)
to
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),V)
via the graded linear isomorphism
\Psi
, we obtain a graded Lie algebra
(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),V),[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{PL}})
as claimed.
∎

Remark 2.16
.

In fact, it is easy to see that
(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),V),\circ)
is a graded pre-Lie algebra.

An ordinary vector space
V
can be naturally viewed as a graded vector space concentrated at degree
-1
. We will see in a moment that in the corresponding graded Lie algebra obtained by Theorem
2.15
‘controls’ post-Lie algebra structures on
V
.

Let
V
be a vector space. Consider the graded vector space

C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{*}(V,V)=\oplus_{n=0}^{+\infty}C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{n}(V,V),

given by the formula

C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{n}(V,V)=\oplus_{i=0}^{n}\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{i}V\otimes\wedge^{n+1-i}V,V).

We will write
f=(f_{0},\ldots,f_{n})\in C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{n}(V,V)
, where
f_{i}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{i}V\otimes\wedge^{n+1-i}V,V)
. For
f\in C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{n}(V,V)
and
g\in C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{m}(V,V)
, define
f\circ g=\big((f\circ g)_{0},\ldots,(f\circ g)_{n+m}\big)\in C^{n+m}_{\mathsf{PL}}(V,V)
for
0\leq k\leq m
by

\displaystyle(f\circ g)_{k}(x_{1}\ldots x_{k}\otimes x_{k+1}\ldots x_{n+m+1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(k-j,j)}\atop 0\leq j\leq k}\sum_{\tau\in\mathbb{S}_{(m+1-j,n+j-k)}}(-1)^{\sigma}(-1)^{\tau}(-1)^{m(k-j)}

\displaystyle f_{k-j}\big(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(k-j)}\otimes g_{j}(x_{\sigma(k-j+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(k)}\otimes x_{k+\tau(1)}\ldots x_{k+\tau(m+1-j)})x_{k+\tau(m+2-j)}\ldots x_{k+\tau(n+m+1-k)}\big),

and for
m+1\leq k\leq m+n
by

\displaystyle(f\circ g)_{k}(x_{1}\ldots x_{k}\otimes x_{k+1}\ldots x_{n+m+1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(j,m+1-j,k-m-1)}\atop 0\leq j\leq m}(-1)^{\sigma}f_{k-m}\big(g_{j}(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(j)}\otimes x_{\sigma(j+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(m+1)})x_{\sigma(m+2)}\ldots x_{\sigma(k)}\otimes x_{k+1}\ldots x_{n+m+1}\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(k-j,j)}\atop 0\leq j\leq m}\sum_{\tau\in\mathbb{S}_{(m+1-j,n+j-k)}}(-1)^{\sigma}(-1)^{\tau}(-1)^{m(k-j)}

\displaystyle f_{k-j}\big(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(k-j)}\otimes g_{j}(x_{\sigma(k-j+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(k)}\otimes x_{k+\tau(1)}\ldots x_{k+\tau(m+1-j)})x_{k+\tau(m+2-j)}\ldots x_{k+\tau(n+m+1-k)}\big).

Theorem 2.17
.

Let
V
be a vector space. Then
(C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{*}(V,V),[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{PL}})
is a graded Lie algebra, where the graded Lie bracket
[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{PL}}
is given by

(25)

[f,g]_{\mathsf{PL}}:=f\circ g-(-1)^{nm}g\circ f,\quad\forall f\in C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{n}(V,V),~g\in C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{m}(V,V).

Moreover, its Maurer-Cartan elements are precisely post-Lie algebra structures on the vector space
V
.

Proof.

The graded Lie algebra structure
[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{PL}}
is deduced from Theorem
2.15
.
For all
\pi=(\pi_{0},\pi_{1})\in C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{1}(V,V)
, where
\pi_{0}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{2}V,V)
and
\pi_{1}\in\mathrm{Hom}(V\otimes V,V)
, we have

[\pi,\pi]_{\mathsf{PL}}=2\pi\circ\pi=2((\pi\circ\pi)_{0},(\pi\circ\pi)_{1},(\pi\circ\pi)_{2}),

where the maps

(\pi\circ\pi)_{0}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{3}V,V),\quad(\pi\circ\pi)_{1}\in\mathrm{Hom}(V\otimes\wedge^{2}V,V),\quad(\pi\circ\pi)_{2}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{2}V\otimes V,V)

are given by

\displaystyle(\pi\circ\pi)_{0}(x_{1}x_{2}x_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{0}(\pi_{0}(x_{1}x_{2})x_{3})-\pi_{0}(\pi_{0}(x_{1}x_{3})x_{2})+\pi_{0}(\pi_{0}(x_{2}x_{3})x_{1}),

\displaystyle(\pi\circ\pi)_{1}(x_{1}\otimes x_{2}x_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\pi_{1}(x_{1}\otimes\pi_{0}(x_{2}x_{3}))+\pi_{0}(\pi_{1}(x_{1}\otimes x_{2})x_{3})-\pi_{0}(\pi_{1}(x_{1}\otimes x_{3})x_{2}),

\displaystyle(\pi\circ\pi)_{2}(x_{1}x_{2}\otimes x_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{1}(\pi_{0}(x_{1}x_{2})\otimes x_{3})+\pi_{1}(\pi_{1}(x_{1}\otimes x_{2})\otimes x_{3})-\pi_{1}(\pi_{1}(x_{2}\otimes x_{1})\otimes x_{3})

\displaystyle-\pi_{1}(x_{1}\otimes\pi_{1}(x_{2}\otimes x_{3}))+\pi_{1}(x_{2}\otimes\pi_{1}(x_{1}\otimes x_{3})).

Therefore,
\pi=(\pi_{0},\pi_{1})\in C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{1}(V,V)
is a Maurer-Cartan element of
(C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{*}(V,V),[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{PL}})
if and only if
(\pi\circ\pi)_{0}=0,~(\pi\circ\pi)_{1}=0,~(\pi\circ\pi)_{2}=0
. Note that
(\pi\circ\pi)_{0}=0
implies that
\pi_{0}
is a Lie algebra structure on
V
,
(\pi\circ\pi)_{1}=0
implies that
\pi_{1}(x_{1},\cdot):V\to V
is a derivation on the Lie algebra
(V,\pi_{0})
, and
(\pi\circ\pi)_{2}=0
implies the compatibility condition (
3
) for
\pi_{0}
and
\pi_{1}
.
Therefore,
\pi=(\pi_{0},\pi_{1})
is a Maurer-Cartan element if and only if
(V,\pi_{0},\pi_{1})
is a post-Lie algebra.
∎

Let
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd)
be a post-Lie algebra. Denote by
\pi=(\pi_{0},\pi_{1})
, where
\pi_{0}=[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\pi_{1}=\rhd
. By Theorem
2.17
,
\pi
is a Maurer-Cartan element of the graded Lie algebra
(C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{*}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}),[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{PL}})
.
Define the inner derivation
d_{\pi}:C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{*}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})\,\rightarrow\,C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{*}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
by

\displaystyle d_{\pi}(f):=[\pi,f]_{\mathsf{PL}},\,\,\,\,\forall f\in C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{*}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}).

By
[\pi,\pi]_{\mathsf{PL}}=0
, we obtain a
dg Lie algebra
(C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{*}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}),[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{PL}},d_{\pi})
. This dg Lie algebra governs deformations of the post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd)
.

Theorem 2.18
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},\pi_{0},\pi_{1})
be a post-Lie algebra. Then
(\mathfrak{g},\pi_{0}+\pi^{\prime}_{0},\pi_{1}+\pi^{\prime}_{1})
is still a post-Lie algebra, where
\pi^{\prime}_{0}:\mathfrak{g}\wedge\mathfrak{g}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}
and
\pi^{\prime}_{1}:\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}
are linear maps, if and only if
\pi^{\prime}=(\pi_{0}^{\prime},\pi_{1}^{\prime})
is a Maurer-Cartan
element of the dg Lie algebra

(C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{*}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}),[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{PL}},d_{\pi})
.

Proof.

It is straightforward.
∎

3.
Cohomologies of post-Lie algebras

In this section, we define the cohomology of a post-Lie algebra using the dg Lie algebra given in Theorem
2.18
. As an application, we classify infinitesimal deformations of a post-Lie algebra using the second cohomology group of the relevant complex.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd)
be a post-Lie algebra, and set
\pi:=(\pi_{0},\pi_{1})
, where
\pi_{0}=[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\pi_{1}=\rhd
. Define the set of
0
-cochains
\mathfrak{C}^{0}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
to be
0
, and define
the set of
n
-cochains
\mathfrak{C}^{n}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
to be
C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{n-1}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
for
~n\geq 1
, i.e.

\mathfrak{C}^{n}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})=\oplus_{i=0}^{n-1}\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{i}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\wedge^{n-i}\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}).

Define the
coboundary operator

\partial:\mathfrak{C}^{n}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{C}^{n+1}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
by

(26)

\partial(f)=(-1)^{n-1}d_{\pi}(f)=(-1)^{n-1}[\pi,f]_{\mathsf{PL}},\,\,\,\,\forall f\in\mathfrak{C}^{n}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}).

Definition 3.1
.

The cohomology of the cochain complex
(\oplus_{n=0}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{C}^{n}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g}),\partial)
is called the
cohomology of the post-Lie algebra

(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd)
. We denote its
n
-th cohomology group by
\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
.

Remark 3.2
.

Since
\mathsf{PostLie}
operad is a Koszul operad
[
51
]
, the operadic cohomology theory of post-Lie algebras introduced here
is isomorphic to the André-Quillen cohomology
[
33
,
43
]
.

To better understand this cohomology, we write the differential
d_{\pi}
more clearly.
For any
f\in C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{n-1}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
, we have

\displaystyle(d_{\pi}(f))_{k}(x_{1}\ldots x_{k}\otimes x_{k+1}\ldots x_{n+1})=(\pi\circ f-(-1)^{n-1}f\circ\pi)_{k}(x_{1}\ldots x_{k}\otimes x_{k+1}\ldots x_{n+1}).

For
k=0
, we have

\displaystyle(\pi\circ f-(-1)^{n-1}f\circ\pi)_{0}(x_{1}\ldots x_{n+1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n+1}(-1)^{n+1-i}[f_{0}(x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots x_{n+1}),x_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle-(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n+1}(-1)^{i+j-1}f_{0}([x_{i},x_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots\hat{x}_{j}\ldots x_{n+1}).

For
k=1
, we have

\displaystyle(\pi\circ f-(-1)^{n-1}f\circ\pi)_{1}(x_{1}\otimes x_{2}\ldots x_{n+1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{n-1}x_{1}\rhd f_{0}(x_{2}\ldots x_{n+1})+\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}(-1)^{n+1-i}[f_{1}(x_{1}\otimes x_{2}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots x_{n+1}),x_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle-(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{2\leq i<j\leq n+1}(-1)^{i+j}f_{1}(x_{1}\otimes[x_{i},x_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{2}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots\hat{x}_{j}\ldots x_{n+1})

\displaystyle-(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}(-1)^{i}f_{0}(x_{1}\rhd x_{i}x_{2}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots x_{n+1}).

For
2\leq k\leq n-1
, we have

\displaystyle(\pi\circ f-(-1)^{n-1}f\circ\pi)_{k}(x_{1}\ldots x_{k}\otimes x_{k+1}\ldots x_{n+1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(-1)^{i-1}x_{i}\rhd f_{k-1}(x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots x_{k}\otimes x_{k+1}\ldots x_{n+1})

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=k+1}^{n+1}(-1)^{n+1-i}[f_{k}(x_{1}\ldots x_{k}\otimes x_{k+1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots x_{n+1}),x_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle-(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq k}(-1)^{i+j-1}f_{k-1}([x_{i},x_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots\hat{x}_{j}\ldots x_{k}\otimes x_{k+1}\ldots x_{n+1})

\displaystyle-(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq k}(-1)^{i+j-1}f_{k-1}\big((x_{i}\rhd x_{j}-x_{j}\rhd x_{i})x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots\hat{x}_{j}\ldots x_{k}\otimes x_{k+1}\ldots x_{n+1}\big)

\displaystyle-(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{k+1\leq i<j\leq n+1}(-1)^{k+i+j-1}f_{k}(x_{1}\ldots x_{k}\otimes[x_{i},x_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{k+1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots\hat{x}_{j}\ldots x_{n+1})

\displaystyle-(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{n+1}(-1)^{i+j+k}f_{k-1}(x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots x_{k}\otimes x_{i}\rhd x_{j}x_{k+1}\ldots\hat{x}_{j}\ldots x_{n+1}).

For
k=n
, we have

\displaystyle(\pi\circ f-(-1)^{n-1}f\circ\pi)_{n}(x_{1}\ldots x_{n}\otimes x_{n+1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(j,n-j)}}(-1)^{\sigma}f_{j}(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(j)}\otimes x_{\sigma(j+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)})\rhd x_{n+1}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{n+i}x_{i}\rhd f_{n-1}(x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots x_{n}\otimes x_{n+1})

\displaystyle-(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}(-1)^{i+j-1}f_{n-1}([x_{i},x_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots\hat{x}_{j}\ldots x_{n}\otimes x_{n+1})

\displaystyle-(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}(-1)^{i+j-1}f_{n-1}\big((x_{i}\rhd x_{j}-x_{j}\rhd x_{i})x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots\hat{x}_{j}\ldots x_{n}\otimes x_{n+1}\big)

\displaystyle-(-1)^{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{i+1}f_{n-1}(x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots x_{n}\otimes x_{i}\rhd x_{n+1}).

We write
\partial=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\partial^{k}
, where
\partial^{k}=\partial|_{\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\wedge^{n-k}\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})}
. Then by the formula of
d_{\pi}
given above, we can write

\partial^{n-1}=\partial^{n-1}_{n-1}+\partial^{n-1}_{n},\quad\partial^{k}=\partial^{k}_{k}+\partial^{k}_{k+1}+\partial^{k}_{n},\quad\mbox{for}~k=0,\ldots,n-2,

where
\partial^{k}_{j}
,
j\in\{k,k+1,n\}
is the map
\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\wedge^{n-k}\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})\to\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{j}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\wedge^{n+1-j}\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
given by the following formulas:

•

for
k=0,1,\cdots,n-1
,

\displaystyle(\partial^{k}_{k}f_{k})(x_{1}\ldots x_{k};x_{k+1}\ldots x_{n+1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k+1}^{n+1}(-1)^{i+1}[x_{i},f_{k}(x_{1}\ldots x_{k};x_{k+1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots x_{n+1})]_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k+1\leq i<j\leq n+1}(-1)^{k+i+j}f_{k}(x_{1}\ldots x_{k};[x_{i},x_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{k+1}\ldots,\hat{x}_{i}\ldots\hat{x}_{j}\ldots x_{n+1}),

•

for
k=0,1,\cdots,n-2
,

\displaystyle(\partial_{k+1}^{k}f_{k})(x_{1}\ldots x_{k+1};x_{k+2}\ldots x_{n+1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{i-1}x_{i}\rhd f_{k}(x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots x_{k+1};x_{k+2}\ldots x_{n+1})

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq k+1}(-1)^{i+j}f_{k}\big(([x_{i},x_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}}+x_{i}\rhd x_{j}-x_{j}\rhd x_{i})x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots\hat{x}_{j}\ldots x_{k+1};x_{k+2}\ldots x_{n+1}\big)

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}\sum_{j=k+2}^{n+1}(-1)^{i+j+k+1}f_{k}(x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots x_{k+1};x_{i}\rhd x_{j}x_{k+2}\ldots\hat{x}_{j}\ldots x_{n+1}),

•

for
k=0,1,\cdots,n-2
,

\displaystyle(\partial_{n}^{k}f_{k})(x_{1}\ldots x_{n};x_{n+1})=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(k,n-k)}}(-1)^{n-1}(-1)^{\sigma}f_{k}(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(k)};x_{\sigma(k+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)})\rhd x_{n+1},

and

\displaystyle(\partial_{n}^{n-1}f_{n-1})(x_{1}\ldots x_{n};x_{n+1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{i+1}x_{i}\rhd f_{n-1}(x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots x_{n};x_{n+1})

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{i+1}f_{n-1}(x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots x_{n};x_{i})\rhd x_{n+1}

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}(-1)^{i+j}f_{n-1}\big(([x_{i},x_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}}+x_{i}\rhd x_{j}-x_{j}\rhd x_{i})x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots\hat{x}_{j}\ldots x_{n};x_{n+1}\big)

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{i+1}f_{n-1}(x_{1}\ldots\hat{x}_{i}\ldots x_{n};x_{i}\rhd x_{n+1}).

We introduce the notations
C^{k,s}=\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\wedge^{s}\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
.
This is illustrated by the following diagram:

\textstyle{C^{0,n-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{\partial}^{0}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{{\partial}^{0}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{{\partial}^{0}_{n-1}}
\textstyle{C^{1,n-2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{\partial}^{1}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{{\partial}^{1}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{{\partial}^{1}_{n-1}}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{C^{n-2,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{\partial}^{n-2}_{n-2}}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\partial}^{n-2}_{n-1}}}

\scriptstyle{{\partial}^{0}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{{\partial}^{0}_{n}}
