\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{\partial}^{1}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{{\partial}^{1}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{{\partial}^{1}_{n}}
\textstyle{C^{2,n-2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{\partial}^{2}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{{\partial}^{2}_{3}}
\scriptstyle{{\partial}^{2}_{n}}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{C^{n-2,2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{\partial}^{n-2}_{n-2}}}
\scriptstyle{{\partial}^{n-2}_{n-1}}
\scriptstyle{{\partial}^{n-2}_{n}}
\textstyle{C^{n-1,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{\partial}^{n-1}_{n-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\partial}^{n-1}_{n}}}
\textstyle{C^{0,n+1}}
\textstyle{C^{1,n}}
\textstyle{C^{2,n-1}}
\textstyle{C^{3,n-2}}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{C^{n-2,3}}
\textstyle{C^{n-1,2}}
\textstyle{C^{n,1}}

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd)
be a post-Lie algebra. For all
k=0,1,2,\cdots
, the maps
\partial^{k}_{k}
induce a differential on
\oplus_{l=1}^{+\infty}\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\wedge^{l}\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
, so that we have the following cochain complex:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial^{k}_{k}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\wedge^{2}\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial^{k}_{k}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\wedge^{3}\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial^{k}_{k}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\cdots.

Proof.

We need to show that
\partial^{k}_{k}\circ\partial^{k}_{k}=0
.
For any
f\in\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
, we have

\displaystyle(\partial\circ\partial)f=\partial(\partial^{k}_{k}f+\partial^{k}_{k+1}f)=\partial^{k}_{k}\partial^{k}_{k}f+\partial^{k}_{k+1}\partial^{k}_{k}f+\partial^{k}_{k+2}\partial^{k}_{k}f+\partial^{k+1}_{k+1}\partial^{k}_{k+1}f+\partial^{k+1}_{k+2}\partial^{k}_{k+1}f=0.

Thus, we deduce that
\partial^{k}_{k}\partial^{k}_{k}f=0,
for all
f\in\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
.

For
m\geq 2
and
g\in\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\wedge^{m}\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
, we have

\displaystyle(\partial\circ\partial)g

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial(\partial^{k}_{k}g+\partial^{k}_{k+1}g+\partial^{k}_{k+m}g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial^{k}_{k}\partial^{k}_{k}g+\partial^{k}_{k+1}\partial^{k}_{k}g+\partial^{k}_{k+m+1}\partial^{k}_{k}g+\partial^{k+1}_{k+1}\partial^{k}_{k+1}g+\partial^{k+1}_{k+2}\partial^{k}_{k+1}g+\partial^{k+1}_{k+m+1}\partial^{k}_{k+1}g

\displaystyle+\partial^{k+m}_{k+m}\partial^{k}_{k+m}g+\partial^{k+m}_{k+m+1}\partial^{k}_{k+m}g

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

which implies that
\partial^{k}_{k}\partial^{k}_{k}g=0,
for all
g\in\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\wedge^{m}\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
.
∎

Remark 3.4
.

By the definition of
\partial^{k}_{k}
, obviously we have
\partial^{0}_{0}=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{CE}}
, the Chevalley-Eilenberg coboundary operator of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
with coefficients in the adjoint representation. Thus the cochain complex
(\oplus_{l=1}^{+\infty}\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\wedge^{l}\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}),\partial^{k}_{k})
can be viewed as a higher version of the Chevalley-Eilenberg complex of the Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}})
.

Now we have seen that the Chevalley-Eilenberg complex of a Lie algebra is a subcomplex of the cochain complex associated to a post-Lie algebra defined above. This is reasonable and expected, since there is a Lie algebra inside a post-Lie algebra. Recall from
[
50
]
that there is another complex of a post-Lie algebra defined in terms of its sub-adjacent Lie algebra. Later in this paper, we indicate the precise relation between the cohomology given in
[
50
]
and the cohomology of a post-Lie algebra defined in Definition
3.1
.

Note that
(\oplus_{n=1}^{+\infty}\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{n-1}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}),\partial^{n-1}_{n})
is not a subcomplex. In fact, for all
f\in\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{n-1}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
, we have

\displaystyle 0=(\partial\circ\partial)f=\partial(\partial^{n-1}_{n-1}f+\partial^{n-1}_{n}f)=\partial^{n-1}_{n-1}\partial^{n-1}_{n-1}f+\partial^{n-1}_{n}\partial^{n-1}_{n-1}f+\partial^{n-1}_{n+1}\partial^{n-1}_{n-1}f+\partial^{n}_{n}\partial^{n-1}_{n}f+\partial^{n}_{n+1}\partial^{n-1}_{n}f,

which implies that

\partial^{n}_{n+1}\partial^{n-1}_{n}f+\partial^{n-1}_{n+1}\partial^{n-1}_{n-1}f=0,\quad\forall f\in\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{n-1}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}).

Thus, the obstruction of
\partial^{n}_{n+1}\partial^{n-1}_{n}f
being zero is given by
\partial^{n-1}_{n+1}\partial^{n-1}_{n-1}f
. So one natural approach to obtain a reduced complex is considering the graded space
\oplus_{n=1}^{+\infty}\ker(\partial^{n-1}_{n-1})\subset\oplus_{n=1}^{+\infty}\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{n-1}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
. By the explicit formula of
\partial^{n-1}_{n-1}
, we have

\displaystyle(\partial^{n-1}_{n-1}f)(x_{1}\ldots x_{n-1};x_{n}x_{n+1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{n+1}[x_{n},f(x_{1}\ldots x_{n-1};x_{n+1})]_{\mathfrak{g}}+(-1)^{n+2}[x_{n+1},f(x_{1}\ldots x_{n-1};x_{n})]_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle+(-1)^{n+2}f(x_{1}\ldots x_{n-1};[x_{n},x_{n+1}]_{\mathfrak{g}}).

In
[
50
]
,
\ker(\partial^{n-1}_{n-1})
is denoted by
C_{\mathsf{Der}}^{n}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
. Now we obtain a reduced complex as follows.

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd)
be a post-Lie algebra. Then
\partial^{*-1}_{*}
determines a differential on
\oplus_{n=1}^{+\infty}\mathsf{Der}^{n}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
so that we have a cochain complex as follows:

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{Der}}^{1}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial^{0}_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}C_{\mathsf{Der}}^{2}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial^{1}_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}C_{\mathsf{Der}}^{3}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial^{2}_{3}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\cdots.

Remark 3.6
.

The cochain complex given in Lemma
3.5
is exactly the one given in
[
50
, Theorem 4.9]
, whose cohomology are used to classify certain operator homotopy post-Lie algebras.

Remark 3.7
.

Besides the higher versions of the Chevalley-Eilenberg cochain complex given in Lemma
3.3
and the reduced cochain complex of Lemma
3.5
, there are other cochain complexes inside the cochain complex of a post-Lie algebra. Similarly to the proof of Lemma
3.3
, it is straightforward to obtain that
(\oplus_{n=1}^{+\infty}\mathrm{Hom}(\wedge^{n-1}\mathfrak{g}\otimes\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}),\partial^{*-1}_{*})
is a cochain complex for all
k=3,4,\cdots.

Next we give applications of the first and the second cohomology group.

Note that
f\in\mathfrak{C}^{1}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
is closed if and only if

(27)

\displaystyle\partial(f)_{0}(x_{1}x_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[x_{1},f(x_{2})]_{\mathfrak{g}}+[f(x_{1}),x_{2}]_{\mathfrak{g}}-f([x_{1},x_{2}]_{\mathfrak{g}})=0,

(28)

\displaystyle\partial(f)_{1}(x_{1}\otimes x_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f(x_{1})\rhd x_{2}+x_{1}\rhd f(x_{2})-f(x_{1}\rhd x_{2})=0.

Therefore, we have the following proposition.

Proposition 3.8
.

A
1
-cochain
f\in\mathfrak{C}^{1}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
is a
1
-cocycle if and only
f
is a derivation. Moreover,
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})=\mathsf{Der}(\mathfrak{g})
.

Now we introduce the notion of an
\mathsf{R}
-deformation of a post-Lie algebra, where
\mathsf{R}
is a local pro-Artinian
{\mathbf{k}}
-algebra. Since
\mathsf{R}
is the projective limit of commutative local Artinian
{\mathbf{k}}
-algebras,
\mathsf{R}
is equipped with an augmentation
\epsilon:\mathsf{R}\,\rightarrow\,{\mathbf{k}}
. See
[
35
]
for more details about
\mathsf{R}
-deformation theory of algebraic structures. An infinitesimal deformation corresponds to the case
\mathsf{R}={\mathbf{k}}[t]/(t^{2})
.

Replacing the
{\mathbf{k}}
-vector spaces and
{\mathbf{k}}
-linear maps by
\mathsf{R}
-modules and
\mathsf{R}
-linear maps in Definition
2.1
and Definition
2.3
, we obtain the definitions of
\mathsf{R}
-post-Lie algebras and homomorphisms between them.

Any post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
can be viewed as an
\mathsf{R}
-post-Lie algebra with the help of the augmentation map
\epsilon
. More precisely, the
\mathsf{R}
-module structure on
\mathfrak{g}
is given by

r\cdot x:=\epsilon(r)x,\quad\forall r\in\mathsf{R},~x\in\mathfrak{g}.

Definition 3.9
.

An
\mathsf{R}
-deformation
of a
{\mathbf{k}}
-post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
consists of an
\mathsf{R}
-post-Lie algebra structure
[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{R}}
on the completed tensor product
\mathsf{R}\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{g}
such that
\epsilon\hat{\otimes}\rm{Id}_{\mathfrak{g}}
is an
\mathsf{R}
-post-Lie algebra homomorphism from
(\mathsf{R}\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{R}},\rhd_{\mathsf{R}})
to
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
.

We denote an
\mathsf{R}
-deformation of
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
by a triple
(\mathsf{R}\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{R}},\rhd_{\mathsf{R}})
.
There is also a natural notion of equivalence between
\mathsf{R}
-deformations.

Definition 3.10
.

Let
(\mathsf{R}\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{R}},\rhd_{\mathsf{R}})
and
(\mathsf{R}\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{R}}^{\prime},\rhd_{\mathsf{R}}^{\prime})
be two
\mathsf{R}
-deformations of a post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
. We call them
equivalent
if there exists an
\mathsf{R}
-post-Lie algebra isomorphism
\phi:(\mathsf{R}\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{R}}^{\prime},\rhd_{\mathsf{R}}^{\prime})\,\rightarrow\,(\mathsf{R}\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{R}},\rhd_{\mathsf{R}})

such that

(29)

\displaystyle\epsilon\hat{\otimes}\rm{Id}_{\mathfrak{g}}=(\epsilon\hat{\otimes}\rm{Id}_{\mathfrak{g}})\circ\phi.

Remark 3.11
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
be a post-Lie algebra. We have the following
deformation functor

\mathsf{Def}_{\mathfrak{g}}:\operatorname{proArt_{loc}}\,\rightarrow\,\mathrm{Set}

which is associated to the equivalent classes of deformations of the post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
. Moreover, the deformation functor
\mathsf{Def}_{\mathfrak{g}}
is isomorphic to
the MC moduli functor
\mathscr{MC}({C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{*}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})},-)
, where
C_{\mathsf{PL}}^{*}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
is the dg Lie algebra given in Theorem
2.18
.

We will now introduce infinitesimal deformations and see that they are classified by the second cohomology group.

Definition 3.12
.

A
{\mathbf{k}}[t]/(t^{2})
-deformation of the
{\mathbf{k}}
-post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
is called an
infinitesimal deformation
.

Note that
{\mathbf{k}}[t]/(t^{2})\hat{\otimes}\mathfrak{g}={\mathbf{k}}[t]/(t^{2})\otimes\mathfrak{g}
. Let
\mathsf{R}={\mathbf{k}}[t]/(t^{2})
and
(\mathsf{R}\otimes\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{R}},\rhd_{\mathsf{R}})
be an infinitesimal deformation of
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
. Since
(\mathsf{R}\otimes\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{R}},\rhd_{\mathsf{R}})
is a post-Lie algebra, there exist
\pi_{0},\omega_{0}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathfrak{g}\wedge\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
and
\pi_{1},\omega_{1}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})
such that

(30)

\displaystyle[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{R}}=\pi_{0}+t\omega_{0},\quad\rhd_{\mathsf{R}}=\pi_{1}+t\omega_{1}.

Since
\epsilon\otimes\rm{Id}_{\mathfrak{g}}
is an
\mathsf{R}
-post-Lie algebra homomorphism from
(\mathsf{R}\otimes\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{R}},\rhd_{\mathsf{R}})
to
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
, we deduce that
\pi_{0}=[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\pi_{1}=\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}
. Therefore, an infinitesimal deformation of
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
is determined by a pair
(\omega_{0},\omega_{1})
.
By the fact that
(\mathsf{R}\otimes\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}}+t\omega_{0})
is an
\mathsf{R}
-Lie algebra, we get

(31)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{CE}}\omega_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

Then by (
2
), we deduce that

(32)

\displaystyle\omega_{1}(x,[y,z]_{\mathfrak{g}})+x\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}\omega_{0}(y,z)-\omega_{0}(x\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}y,z)

\displaystyle-[\omega_{1}(x,y),z]_{\mathfrak{g}}-\omega_{0}(y,x\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}z)-[y,\omega_{1}(x,z)]_{\mathfrak{g}}=0.

Finally by (
3
), we obtain that

(33)

\displaystyle\big(\omega_{1}(x,y)-\omega_{1}(y,x)+\omega_{0}(x,y)\big)\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}z+\omega_{1}\big((x\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}y-y\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}x+[x,y]_{\mathfrak{g}}),z\big)

\displaystyle-\omega_{1}(x,y\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}z)-x\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}\omega_{1}(y,z)+\omega_{1}(y,x\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}z)+y\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}\omega_{1}(x,z)=0.

Theorem 3.13
.

The pair
(\omega_{0},\omega_{1})
determines an infinitesimal deformation of the post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
if and only if
(\omega_{0},\omega_{1})
is a
2
-cocycle of the post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
.

Moreover, there is a one-to-one correspondence between equivalence classes of infinitesimal deformations of the post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
and the second cohomology group
\mathcal{H}^{2}(\mathfrak{g};\mathfrak{g})
.

Proof.

By (
31
), (
32
) and (
33
),
(\omega_{0},\omega_{1})
determines an infinitesimal deformation of the post-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
if and only if
(\omega_{0},\omega_{1})
is a
2
-cocycle.

If two infinitesimal deformations determined by
(\omega_{0},\omega_{1})
and
(\omega_{0}^{\prime},\omega_{1}^{\prime})
are equivalent, then there exists an
\mathsf{R}
-post-Lie algebra isomorphism
\phi
from
(\mathsf{R}\otimes\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}}+t\omega_{0}^{\prime},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}+t\omega_{1}^{\prime})
to
(\mathsf{R}\otimes\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}}+t\omega_{0},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}+t\omega_{1})
. By (
29
), we deduce that

(34)

\displaystyle\phi={\rm{Id}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+tf,\quad\mbox{where}\quad f\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}).

Since
{\rm{Id}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+tf
is an isomorphism from
(\mathsf{R}\otimes\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}}+t\omega_{0}^{\prime})
to
(\mathsf{R}\otimes\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}}+t\omega_{0})
, we get

(35)

\omega_{0}^{\prime}-\omega_{0}=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{CE}}f.

By the equality

({\rm{Id}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+tf)\big(x\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}y+t\omega_{1}^{\prime}(x,y)\big)=\big(({\rm{Id}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+tf)x\big)\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}\big(({\rm{Id}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+tf)y\big)+t\omega_{1}\big(({\rm{Id}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+tf)x,({\rm{Id}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+tf)y\big),

we deduce that

(36)

\omega_{1}^{\prime}(x,y)-\omega_{1}(x,y)=f(x)\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}y+x\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}f(y)-f(x\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}}y).

By (
35
) and (
36
), we deduce that

(\omega_{0}^{\prime},\omega_{1}^{\prime})-(\omega_{0},\omega_{1})=\partial(f),

which implies that
(\omega_{0}^{\prime},\omega_{1}^{\prime})
and
(\omega_{0},\omega_{1})
are in the same cohomology class if and only if the corresponding infinitesimal deformations of
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\rhd_{\mathfrak{g}})
are equivalent.
∎

4.
Post-Lie
∞
algebras

In this section, we introduce the notion of post-Lie
∞
algebras using the graded Lie algebra given in Theorem
2.15
. There is an equivalent characterization of post-Lie
∞
algebras via open-closed homotopy Lie algebras. Various examples of post-Lie
∞
algebras are constructed from higher geometric structures, e.g. homotopy Poisson structures and
L_{\infty}
-algebroids.

4.1.
Post-Lie
∞
algebras and open-closed homotopy Lie algebras

Recall that for a graded vector space
V
there is a symmetric coalgebra
\mathsf{S}(V)
(endowed with the cocommutative shuffle coproduct).
A linear map
D:\mathsf{S}(V)\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{S}(V)
of degree
n
is called a
coderivation of degree
n
on the coalgebra
(\mathsf{S}(V),\Delta,\varepsilon)
if

(37)

\displaystyle\Delta\circ D=(D\otimes{\rm{Id}}+{\rm{Id}}\otimes D)\circ\Delta.

Denote the vector space of coderivations of degree
n
by
\mathsf{coDer}^{n}(\mathsf{S}(V))
. Then
\mathsf{coDer}(\mathsf{S}(V)):=\oplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathsf{coDer}^{n}(\mathsf{S}(V))
has a graded Lie algebra structure as follows:

\displaystyle[D_{1},D_{2}]_{C}:=D_{1}\circ D_{2}-(-1)^{D_{1}D_{2}}D_{2}\circ D_{1},\,\,\forall D_{1},D_{2}\in\mathsf{coDer}(\mathsf{S}(V)).

Denote by
\mathrm{Hom}^{n}(\mathsf{S}(V),V)
the space of total degree
n
linear maps from
\mathsf{S}(V)
to
V
. An element
f\in\mathrm{Hom}^{n}(\mathsf{S}(V),V)
is the sum of its components
f_{i}:\mathsf{S}^{i}(V)\,\rightarrow\,V
so that
f=\sum_{i=0}^{+\infty}f_{i}
. Moreover, we can write any homomorphism
S(V)\to V
as a sum of its graded components:

\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}(V),V)=\oplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{Hom}^{n}(\mathsf{S}(V),V).

Since
(\mathsf{S}(V),\Delta,\varepsilon)
is the cofree object in the category of conilpotent cocommutative coalgebras, we deduce that
\mathsf{coDer}(\mathsf{S}(V))\cong\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}(V),V)
. More precisely, we have the isomorphism
\Psi:\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}(V),V)\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{coDer}(\mathsf{S}(V))
of graded vector spaces, which is given by

(38)

\displaystyle\Psi(f)(v_{1}\ldots v_{n})=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,n-i)}}\varepsilon(\sigma;v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})f_{i}\big(v_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,v_{\sigma(i)}\big)v_{\sigma(i+1)}\ldots v_{\sigma(n)},

here
n=0,1,\ldots,
and
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\in V.
We will denote
\Psi(f)
by
\hat{f}
.

Transferring the graded Lie algebra structure
\big(\mathsf{coDer}(\mathsf{S}(V)),[\cdot,\cdot]_{C}\big)
to
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}(V),V)
via the graded linear isomorphism
\Psi
, we obtain a graded Lie algebra
(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}(V),V),[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{NR}})
. The graded Lie bracket
[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{NR}}
, the
Nijenhuis-Richardson bracket
, is given by the formula

\displaystyle[f,g]_{\mathsf{NR}}:=f\circ g-(-1)^{mn}g\circ f,\,\,\,\,\forall f=\sum_{i=0}^{+\infty}f_{i}\in\mathrm{Hom}^{m}(\mathsf{S}(V),V),~g=\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}g_{j}\in\mathrm{Hom}^{n}(\mathsf{S}(V),V),

where
f\circ g\in\mathrm{Hom}^{m+n}(\mathsf{S}(V),V)
is defined by

\displaystyle f\circ g

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{+\infty}f_{i}\Big)\circ\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}g_{j}\Big):=\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\Big(\sum_{i+j=k+1}f_{i}\circ g_{j}\Big),

while
f_{i}\circ g_{j}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}^{i+j-1}(V),V)
is defined by

\displaystyle(f_{i}\circ g_{j})(v_{1}\ldots v_{i+j-1}):=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(j,i-1)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)f_{i}(g_{j}(v_{\sigma(1)}\ldots v_{\sigma(j)})v_{\sigma(j+1)}\ldots v_{\sigma(i+j-1)}).

It is clear that
(\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{\mathsf{S}}(V),V),[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{NR}})
is a graded Lie subalgebra of
(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}(V),V),[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{NR}})
.

Definition 4.1
.

(
[
29
]
)
An
L_{\infty}
-algebra
structure on a graded vector space
\mathfrak{g}
is a Maurer-Cartan element
\sum_{i=1}^{+\infty}l_{i}
of the graded Lie algebra
(\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}),\mathfrak{g}),[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{NR}})
. More precisely, an
L_{\infty}
-algebra is a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded vector space
\mathfrak{g}=\oplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{g}^{k}
equipped with a collection
(k\geq 1)
of linear maps
l_{k}:\otimes^{k}\mathfrak{g}\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{g}
of degree
1
with the property that, for any homogeneous elements
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in\mathfrak{g}
, we have

(i)

(graded symmetry)
for every
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{n}
,

\displaystyle l_{n}(x_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,x_{\sigma(n-1)},x_{\sigma(n)})=\varepsilon(\sigma)l_{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n-1},x_{n}),

(ii)

(generalized Jacobi identity)
for all
n\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,n-i)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)l_{n-i+1}(l_{i}(x_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,x_{\sigma(i)}),x_{\sigma(i+1)},\ldots,x_{\sigma(n)})=0.

We will write
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
for a graded vector space
\mathfrak{g}
together with an
L_{\infty}
-algebra structure on it.

There is a canonical way to view a dg Lie algebra as an
L_{\infty}
-algebra.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},d)
be a dg Lie algebra. Then
(s^{-1}\mathfrak{g},\{l_{i}\}_{i=1}^{+\infty})
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra, where
l_{1}(s^{-1}x)=-s^{-1}d(x),~l_{2}(s^{-1}x,s^{-1}y)=(-1)^{x}s^{-1}[x,y]_{\mathfrak{g}},~l_{k}=0,
for all
k\geq 3,

and homogeneous elements
x,y\in\mathfrak{g}
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
be an
L_{\infty}
-algebra. Then
(\mathsf{S}(\mathfrak{g}),\Delta,\Psi(\sum_{i=1}^{+\infty}l_{i}))
is a dg coalgebra. Homomorphisms between
L_{\infty}
-algebras can be defined in term of corresponding dg coalgebra structures.

Definition 4.3
.

(
[
27
]
)
Let
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
and
(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime},\{l_{k}^{\prime}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
be two
L_{\infty}
-algebras.
An
L_{\infty}
-algebra
homomorphism
from
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
to
(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime},\{l_{k}^{\prime}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
consists of a collection of degree
0
graded multilinear maps
f_{k}:\mathfrak{g}^{\otimes k}\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{g}^{\prime},~k\geq 1
with the property

f_{n}(v_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,v_{\sigma(n)})=\varepsilon(\sigma)f_{n}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}),
for any
n\geq 1
and homogeneous elements
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\in\mathfrak{g}
,
and

(39)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,n-i)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)f_{n-i+1}\Big(l_{i}(v_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,v_{\sigma(i)}),v_{\sigma(i+1)},\ldots,v_{\sigma(n)}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k_{1}+\ldots+k_{i}=n}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{i})}}\frac{\varepsilon(\sigma)}{i!}l_{i}^{\prime}\Big(f_{k_{1}}(v_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,v_{\sigma(k_{1})}),\ldots,f_{k_{i}}(v_{\sigma(k_{1}+\ldots+k_{i-1}+1)},\ldots,v_{\sigma(n)})\Big).

Equivalently,
\bar{f}:\mathsf{S}(\mathfrak{g})\to\mathsf{S}(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})
is a homomorphism of dg coalgebras, where
f=\sum_{k=1}^{+\infty}f_{k}
and
\bar{f}
is given by (
1
).

Now we are ready to introduce the notion of a post-Lie
∞
algebra using the graded Lie algebra given in Theorem
2.15
.

Definition 4.4
.

A
post-Lie
∞
algebra
structure on a graded vector space
\mathfrak{g}
is defined to be a Maurer-Cartan element
\sum_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1}\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}
of the graded Lie algebra
(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{S}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}),\mathfrak{g}),[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathsf{PL}})
. More precisely,
a post-Lie
∞
algebra is a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded vector space
\mathfrak{g}=\oplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{g}^{k}
equipped with a collection of linear maps
\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}:\mathsf{S}^{p}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes\mathsf{S}^{q}(\mathfrak{g})\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{g}
of degree
1
for
p\geq 0,q\geq 1
satisfying, for every collection of
homogeneous elements
x_{1},\ldots,x_{m+n}\in\mathfrak{g}
, the following identities:

(40)

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(j,i,n-j-i)}\atop 0\leq j\leq n-1,1\leq i\leq n}\varepsilon(\sigma)\mathfrak{M}_{n-j-i+1,m}\Big(\mathfrak{M}_{j,i}(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(j)}\otimes x_{\sigma(j+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(j+i)})x_{\sigma(j+i+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)}\otimes x_{n+1}\ldots x_{n+m}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(n-j,j)}\atop 0\leq j\leq n}\sum_{\tau\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,m-i)}\atop 1\leq i\leq m}\varepsilon(\sigma)\varepsilon(\tau)(-1)^{x_{\sigma(1)}+\ldots+x_{\sigma(n-j)}}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{n-j,m-i+1}\Big(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n-j)}\otimes\mathfrak{M}_{j,i}\big(x_{\sigma(n-j+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)}\otimes x_{n+\tau(1)}\ldots x_{n+\tau(i)}\big)x_{n+\tau(i+1)}\ldots x_{n+\tau(m)}\Big)

\displaystyle=0,

for
n\geq 0,m\geq 1.

Several remarks are in order to illustrate the relation between post-Lie
∞
algebras,
L_{\infty}
-algebras, pre-Lie
∞
algebras and operator homotopy post-Lie algebras.

Remark 4.5
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1})
be a post-Lie
∞
algebra. Setting
n=0
in (
40
), we obtain that
\{\mathfrak{M}_{0,q}\}_{q\geq 1}
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra
structure on the graded vector space
\mathfrak{g}
.

Remark 4.6
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1})
be a post-Lie
∞
algebra that satisfies
\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}=0,q\geq 2
. For all
n\geq 1
, we define
\theta_{n}:\otimes^{n}\mathfrak{g}\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{g},n\geq 1,
by

(41)

\displaystyle\theta_{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\triangleq\mathfrak{M}_{n-1,1}(x_{1}\ldots x_{n-1}\otimes x_{n}).

Then
(\mathfrak{g},\{\theta_{n}\}_{n\geq 1})
is a
pre-Lie
∞
algebra
, which was introduced in
[
14
]
. See
[
39
]
for more applications of pre-Lie
∞
algebras in geometry.

Remark 4.7
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1})
be a post-Lie
∞
algebra that satisfies
\mathfrak{M}_{0,q}=0,q\geq 3
and
\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}=0,p\geq 1,q\geq 2
. We define
[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}}:\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{g}
by

[x,y]_{\mathfrak{g}}\triangleq\mathfrak{M}_{0,2}(xy),
for all
x,y\in\mathfrak{g}.

For all
n\geq 1
, we define
\theta_{n}:\otimes^{n}\mathfrak{g}\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{g},n\geq 1,
by

(42)

\displaystyle\theta_{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\triangleq\mathfrak{M}_{n-1,1}(x_{1}\ldots x_{n-1}\otimes x_{n}).

Then
(\mathfrak{g},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}},\{\theta_{n}\}_{n\geq 1})
is an
operator homotopy post-Lie algebra
, which was introduced in
[
50
]
and the first attempt to study the homotopy theory of post-Lie algebras.

Now we recall the notion of an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra, and use open-closed homotopy Lie algebras to characterize post-Lie
∞
algebras.

Definition 4.8
.

(
[
15
,
37
,
40
]
)
An
open-closed homotopy Lie algebra

(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
consists of an
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
and
a family of graded linear maps
\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}:\mathsf{S}^{p}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes\mathsf{S}^{q}(\mathfrak{h})\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{h}
of degree
1
for
p\geq 0,q\geq 0
with
p+q\geq 1
satisfying the following compatibility conditions:

(43)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,n-i)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)\mathfrak{R}_{n-i+1,m}\Big(l_{i}(x_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,x_{\sigma(i)})x_{\sigma(i+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)}\otimes u_{1}\ldots u_{m}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=0}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,n-i)}}\sum_{\tau\in\mathbb{S}_{(k,m-k)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)\varepsilon(\tau)(-1)^{x_{\sigma(1)}+\ldots+x_{\sigma(i)}}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{i,m-k+1}\Big(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(i)}\otimes\mathfrak{R}_{n-i,k}\big(x_{\sigma(i+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)}\otimes u_{\tau(1)}\ldots u_{\tau(k)}\big)u_{\tau(k+1)}\ldots u_{\tau(m)}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

for
n+m\geq 1
and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in\mathfrak{g},u_{1},\ldots,u_{m}\in\mathfrak{h}.

Remark 4.9
.

When
n=0
, (
43
) implies that
\{\mathfrak{R}_{0,q}\}_{q=1}^{+\infty}
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra structure on the graded vector space
\mathfrak{h}
. Moreover, if
\mathfrak{R}_{p,0}=0
for all
p\geq 1
, then an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
reduces to an
L_{\infty}
-action of the
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
on the
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathfrak{h},\{\mathfrak{R}_{0,q}\}_{q=1}^{+\infty})
. See
[
12
, Definition 2.4]
for more details about
L_{\infty}
-actions of
L_{\infty}
-algebras.
In general, an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra is equivalent to a (nonabelian) extension of
L_{\infty}
-algebras. In particular, there is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra structure
\{\mathcal{R}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
on
\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h}
given by

(44)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{k}\big((x_{1},u_{1}),\ldots,(x_{k},u_{k})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(l_{k}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}),\sum_{i=0}^{k}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,k-i)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)\mathfrak{R}_{i,k-i}(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(i)}\otimes u_{\sigma(i+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(k)})\Big).

One can say that the
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h},\{\mathcal{R}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
is an extension of
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
by
(\mathfrak{h},\{\mathfrak{R}_{0,q}\}_{q=1}^{+\infty})
.

Now we give an explicit equivalent description of open-closed homotopy Lie algebras. Let
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
and
(\mathfrak{h},\{\mu_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
be
L_{\infty}
-algebras, and
\{{\rho_{k}}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
be a graded linear map of degree
0
from
\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g})
to
s^{-1}\mathsf{coDer}(\mathsf{S}(\mathfrak{h}))
. Define
\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}:\mathsf{S}^{p}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes\mathsf{S}^{q}(\mathfrak{h})\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{h}
by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}(x_{1}\ldots x_{p}\otimes u_{1}\ldots u_{q}):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mu_{q}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{q}),&\mbox{$p=0,q\geq 1$, }\\
\mathrm{pr}_{\mathfrak{h}}\big((s\rho_{p})(x_{1},\ldots,x_{p})(u_{1},\ldots,u_{q})\big),&\mbox{$p\geq 1,q\geq 0$.}\end{array}\right.

Theorem 4.10
.

(
[
31
,
37
]
)

With the above notations,
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
is an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra if and only if
\{{\rho_{k}}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra homomorphism from
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
to
s^{-1}\mathsf{coDer}(\mathsf{S}(\mathfrak{h}))
.

Remark 4.11
.

An
L_{\infty}
-action of an
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
on an
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathfrak{h},\{\mu_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
is equivalent to an
L_{\infty}
-algebra homomorphism
\rho=\{\rho_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
from
\mathfrak{g}
to
s^{-1}\mathsf{coDer}(\overline{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h}))
. Such an action is, therefore, a special case of an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra.

Theorem 4.12
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1})
be a post-Lie
∞
algebra. Define
l^{C}_{k}
by

(48)

\displaystyle l^{C}_{k}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\triangleq\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(j,k-j)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)\mathfrak{M}_{j,k-j}(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(j)}\otimes x_{\sigma(j+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(k)}),

for all
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\in\mathfrak{g}
. Then
(\mathfrak{g},\{l^{C}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra, which is denoted by
\mathfrak{g}^{C}
and called the
sub-adjacent
L_{\infty}
-algebra
of the post-Lie
∞
algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1})
.

Moreover,
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g},\{l^{C}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
is an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra such that
\mathfrak{M}_{p,0}=0
for all
p\geq 1
.

Proof.

By Definition
4.4
and Proposition
2.14
,
\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1}
is a post-Lie
∞
algebra structure on
\mathfrak{g}
if and only if
m=\sum_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1}\widehat{\mathfrak{M}}_{p,q}
is a differential of the cocommutative cotrialgebra
(\mathsf{S}(V)\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(V),\Delta,\delta)
. By the linear duality, we also obtain that
\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1}
is a post-Lie
∞
algebra structure on
\mathfrak{g}
if and only if

m^{*}
is a continuous differential on the free complete commutative trialgebra
\widehat{\mathsf{ComTrias}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
. Note that an
L_{\infty}
-algebra structure on
\mathfrak{g}
is a continuous differential
[
31
]
on the free complete nonunital commutative algebra
\widehat{\bar{\mathsf{S}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
. We will now show how to construct a continuous differential on
\widehat{\bar{\mathsf{S}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
from
m^{*}
.

Denote the category of graded vector spaces by
\mathsf{Vect}
, the category of graded nonunital commutative algebras by
\mathsf{Com}
and the category of commutative trialgebras by
\mathsf{ComTrias}
. There is an embedding functor
\mathfrak{i}:\mathsf{Com}\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{ComTrias}
, which is defined by
\mathfrak{i}(A,\cdot):=(A,\cdot,\cdot)
. Let
(A,\cdot,\ast)
be a graded commutative trialgebra. We consider the
2
-sided ideal
I
of the graded commutative trialgebra
(A,\cdot,\ast)
which is generated by all homogeneous elements of the form

x\cdot y-x\ast y,~\forall x,y\in A.

Then the quotient algebra
A/I
is a graded commutative algebra and we denote it by
A_{\mathsf{com}}
. We have constructed a functor

(-)_{\mathsf{com}}:\mathsf{ComTrias}\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{Com}.

By a direct calculation we deduce that
(-)_{\mathsf{com}}
is left adjoint to
\mathfrak{i}
. Since the free commutative trialgebra functor is left adjoint to the forgetful functor from
\mathsf{ComTrias}
to
\mathsf{Vect}
, we conclude that
\mathsf{ComTrias}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})_{\mathsf{com}}
is isomorphic to the free nonunital commutative algebra
\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
. By completion, we obtain that
\widehat{\mathsf{ComTrias}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})_{\mathsf{com}}\cong\widehat{\bar{\mathsf{S}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*}).

Moreover, let
d
be a continuous derivation of
\widehat{\mathsf{ComTrias}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
. We have:

\displaystyle d(x\cdot y-x\ast y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d(x)\cdot y+(-1)^{xd}x\cdot d(y)-\big(d(x)\ast y+(-1)^{xd}x\ast d(y)\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d(x)\cdot y-d(x)\ast y+(-1)^{xd}\big(x\cdot d(y)-x\ast d(y)\big).

Therefore, we obtain that
d(I)\subset I
, which implies that
d
restricts to a unique continuous derivation
\widetilde{d}
on
\widehat{\bar{\mathsf{S}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
. More precisely,
\widetilde{d}:\widehat{\bar{\mathsf{S}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})\,\rightarrow\,\widehat{\bar{\mathsf{S}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
is given by

(49)

\displaystyle\widetilde{d}([x]):=[d(x)],\,\quad\forall d\in\mathsf{Der}(\widehat{\mathsf{ComTrias}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})),~x\in\widehat{\mathsf{ComTrias}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*}).

Furthermore, given
v\in\widehat{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
and
v^{\prime}\in\widehat{\bar{\mathsf{S}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
we have, modulo the relation
*=\cdot
:

\displaystyle 1\otimes vv^{\prime}=(1\otimes v)\cdot(1\otimes v^{\prime})=(1\otimes v)*(1\otimes v^{\prime})=v\otimes v^{\prime}

and similarly
1\otimes vv^{\prime}=(-1)^{|v||v^{\prime}|}1\otimes v^{\prime}v=(-1)^{|v||v^{\prime}|}v^{\prime}\otimes v
. It follows that we have the following isomorphism of commutative trialgebras

(50)

[\widehat{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})\otimes\widehat{\bar{\mathsf{S}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})]/I\cong\prod_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(n,m)}\atop n\geq 0,m\geq 1}[\widehat{\mathsf{S}}^{n}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})\otimes\widehat{\bar{\mathsf{S}}^{m}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})]_{\sigma}\cong\widehat{\bar{\mathsf{S}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*}).

The structure maps
\{l^{C}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
of the corresponding sub-adjacent
L_{\infty}
algebra are dual to the induced differential
\tilde{d}
on
\widehat{\bar{\mathsf{S}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
.
It follows from (
50
) that the dual map

\mathsf{S}(\mathfrak{g})\to\mathsf{S}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g})
has the form

x_{1}\ldots x_{k}\mapsto\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(j,k-j)}}\epsilon(\sigma)(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(j)})\otimes(x_{\sigma(j+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(k)})

which implies that the maps
\{l^{C}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
are as stated (the above argument is similar to deducing the structure maps of the
L_{\infty}
-algebra associated to an
A_{\infty}
-algebra).

A similar, but simpler, argument, shows that the collection of tensors
(\mathfrak{g},\{\mathfrak{M}_{0,q}\}_{q\geq 1})
forms an
L_{\infty}
-algebra. Indeed, in the dual language this corresponds to setting
*=0
in the free commutative trialgebra
\widehat{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})\otimes\widehat{\bar{\mathsf{S}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
and observing that the resulting structure is a square zero derivation of
\widehat{\bar{\mathsf{S}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
, that is an
L_{\infty}
-structure on the graded vector space
\mathfrak{g}
.

Finally, by (
48
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,n-i)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)\mathfrak{M}_{n-i+1,m}\Big(l^{C}_{i}(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(i)}),x_{\sigma(i+1)},\ldots,x_{\sigma(n)}\otimes x_{n+1}\ldots x_{n+m}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=0}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,n-i)}}\sum_{\tau\in\mathbb{S}_{(k,m-k)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)\varepsilon(\tau)(-1)^{x_{\sigma(1)}+\ldots+x_{\sigma(i)}}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{i,m-k+1}\Big(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(i)}\otimes\mathfrak{M}_{n-i,k}\big(x_{\sigma(i+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)}\otimes x_{n+\tau(1)}\ldots x_{n+\tau(k)}\big),x_{n+\tau(k+1)}\ldots x_{n+\tau(m)}\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{sub-homotopy-lie}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,n-i)}}\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\sum_{\tau\in\mathbb{S}_{(j,i-j)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)\varepsilon(\tau)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{n-i+1,m}\Big(\mathfrak{M}_{j,i-j}(x_{\sigma(\tau(1))}\ldots x_{\sigma(\tau(j))}\otimes x_{\sigma(\tau(j+1))}\ldots x_{\sigma(\tau(k))}),x_{\sigma(i+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)}\otimes x_{n+1}\ldots x_{n+m}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=0}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,n-i)}}\sum_{\tau\in\mathbb{S}_{(k,m-k)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)\varepsilon(\tau)(-1)^{x_{\sigma(1)}+\ldots+x_{\sigma(i)}}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{i,m-k+1}\Big(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(i)}\otimes\mathfrak{M}_{n-i,k}\big(x_{\sigma(i+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)}\otimes x_{n+\tau(1)}\ldots x_{n+\tau(k)}\big),x_{n+\tau(k+1)},\ldots,x_{n+\tau(m)}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(j,i,n-j-i)}\atop 0\leq j\leq n-1,1\leq i\leq n}\varepsilon(\sigma)\mathfrak{M}_{n-j-i+1,m}\Big(\mathfrak{M}_{j,i}(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(j)}\otimes x_{\sigma(j+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(j+i)})x_{\sigma(j+i+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)}\otimes x_{n+1}\ldots x_{n+m}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(n-j,j)}\atop 0\leq j\leq n}\sum_{\tau\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,m-i)}\atop 1\leq i\leq m}\varepsilon(\sigma)\varepsilon(\tau)(-1)^{x_{\sigma(1)}+\ldots+x_{\sigma(n-j)}}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{n-j,m-i+1}\Big(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n-j)}\otimes\mathfrak{M}_{j,i}\big(x_{\sigma(n-j+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(n)}\otimes x_{n+\tau(1)}\ldots x_{n+\tau(i)}\big)x_{n+\tau(i+1)}\ldots x_{n+\tau(m)}\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{homotopy-post-lie}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle 0.

Thus,
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g},\{l^{C}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
is an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra such that
\mathfrak{M}_{p,0}=0
for all
p\geq 1
.
∎

Theorem 4.13
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a graded vector space and
\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}:\mathsf{S}^{p}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes\mathsf{S}^{q}(\mathfrak{g})\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{g},~p\geq 0,q\geq 1
be a family of graded linear maps of degree
1
.
Then
(\mathfrak{g},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1})
is a post-Lie
∞
algebra if and only if

(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g},\{l^{C}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
is an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra which satisfies

\displaystyle l^{C}_{k}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(j,k-j)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)\mathfrak{M}_{j,k-j}(x_{\sigma(1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(j)}\otimes x_{\sigma(j+1)}\ldots x_{\sigma(k)}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{p,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,\,\,\,\forall p\geq 1.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.12
, we obtain that
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g},\{l^{C}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
is an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra satisfying
\mathfrak{M}_{p,0}=0
for all
p\geq 1
.

Conversely, by the proof of Theorem
4.12
, we deduce that the equation (
43
) is precisely the equation (
40
). Then we obtain that
(\mathfrak{g},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1})
is a post-Lie
∞
algebra.
∎

4.2.
Post-Lie
∞
algebras and higher geometric structures

Definition 4.14
.

(
[
26
]
)
Let
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
be an
L_{\infty}
-algebra and
(A,\cdot,d)
be a dg commutative algebra. An
action
of the
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
on the dg commutative algebra
(A,\cdot,d)
is a family of homomorphisms
\{\rho_{k}:\mathsf{S}^{k}(\mathfrak{g})\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{Der}(A)\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
of graded vector spaces of degree
1

for any
k\geq 1
satisfying, for every collection of
homogeneous elements
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in\mathfrak{g}
,

(51)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,n-i)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)\rho_{n-i+1}\Big(l_{i}(x_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,x_{\sigma(i)}),x_{\sigma(i+1)},\ldots,x_{\sigma(n)}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i,n-i)}}(-1)^{x_{\sigma(1)}+\cdots+x_{\sigma(i)}}\rho_{i}(x_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,x_{\sigma(i)})\circ\rho_{n-i}(v_{\sigma(k_{1}+\ldots+k_{i-1}+1)},\ldots,v_{\sigma(n)})\Big)

\displaystyle+d\circ\rho_{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})-(-1)^{x_{1}+\cdots+x_{n}+1}\rho_{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\circ d

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

Equivalently,
\{s^{-1}\circ\rho_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra homomorphism form
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
to
s^{-1}\mathsf{Der}(A)
.

Theorem 4.15
.

Let
\{\rho_{k}:\mathsf{S}^{k}(\mathfrak{g})\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{Der}(A)\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
be an action of an
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
on a dg commutative algebra
(A,\cdot,d)
. Then there is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra structure on
A\otimes\mathfrak{g}
, which is defined by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{l}_{1}(a_{1}\otimes x_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d(a_{1})\otimes x_{1}+(-1)^{a_{1}}a_{1}\otimes l_{1}(x_{1}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{l}_{k}(a_{1}\otimes x_{1},\ldots,a_{k}\otimes x_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{k}(1+x_{1}+\ldots+x_{i-1})a_{i}}a_{1}\ldots a_{k}\otimes l_{k}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{k}(-1)^{(1+x_{1}+\ldots+x_{j-1}+a_{j+1}+x_{j+1}+\ldots+a_{k}+x_{k})a_{j}+(x_{j+1}+\ldots+x_{k})x_{j}}

\displaystyle(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{k}(1+x_{1}+\ldots+x_{i-1})a_{i}}a_{1}\ldots\hat{a}_{j}\ldots a_{k}\rho_{k-1}(x_{1},\ldots,\hat{x}_{j},\ldots,x_{k})(a_{j})\otimes x_{j}.

It is called the action
L_{\infty}
-algebra of
\{\rho_{k}:\mathsf{S}^{k}(\mathfrak{g})\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{Der}(A)\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
.

For the proof (though not for a formulation) we will need the notion of an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
, also known as a homotopy Lie-Rinehart algebra. Recall from
[
26
,
55
]

that an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid over a graded commutative algebra
{A}
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
together with an
{A}
-module structure on
\mathfrak{g}
and an action
\{\rho_{k}:\mathsf{S}^{k}(\mathfrak{g})\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{Der}(A)\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
(called the anchor) such that

\displaystyle\rho(ax_{1},\ldots,x_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{a}a\rho(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}),

\displaystyle l_{k+1}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k},ax_{k+1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho_{k}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})(a)x_{k+1}+(-1)^{a(1+x_{1}+\ldots+x_{k})}al_{k+1}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+1}),

here
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k+1}\in\mathfrak{g},~a\in{A}.
We usually denote an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid over
{A}
by
({A},\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\rho_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
.

Proof of Theorem
4.15
.

The given an action
\{\rho_{k}:\mathsf{S}^{k}(\mathfrak{g})\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{Der}(A)\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
gives rise to the structure of an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid on the pair
(A,A\otimes\mathfrak{g})
, cf.
[
52
, Corollary 38]
where this statement is proved in a more general situation when
\mathfrak{g}
is itself an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid acting on
A
. A straightforward computation shows that the structure maps of this
L_{\infty}
-algebroid are as stated. Since an
L_{\infty}
-algebra is an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid of a special kind, the desired statement follows.
∎

Let
\{\rho_{k}:\mathsf{S}^{k}(\mathfrak{g})\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{Der}(A)\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
be an action as above. We define the graded linear map

\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}:\mathsf{S}^{p}(A\otimes\mathfrak{g})\otimes\mathsf{S}^{q}(A\otimes\mathfrak{g})\,\rightarrow\,A\otimes\mathfrak{g}

by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{0,1}(a_{1}\otimes x_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d(a_{1})\otimes x_{1}+(-1)^{a_{1}}a_{1}\otimes l_{1}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{0,q}(a_{1}\otimes x_{1}\ldots a_{q}\otimes x_{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{q}(1+x_{1}+\ldots+x_{i-1})a_{i}}a_{1}\ldots a_{q}\otimes l_{q}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{q})

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{p,1}\big((a_{1}\otimes x_{1}\ldots a_{p}\otimes x_{p})\otimes(a_{p+1}\otimes x_{p+1})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{p}(1+x_{1}+\ldots+x_{i-1})a_{i}}a_{1}\ldots a_{p}\rho_{p}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{p})(a_{p+1})\otimes x_{p+1},

and

\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}=0
for other cases.

Theorem 4.16
.

With the notation as above,
(A\otimes\mathfrak{g},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1})
is a post-Lie
∞
algebra, whose sub-adjacent
L_{\infty}
-algebra is exactly the action
L_{\infty}
-algebra.

Proof.

By (
48
), we note that
(A\otimes\mathfrak{g},\{l^{C}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
is the action
L_{\infty}
-algebra. Since
\{\rho_{k}:\mathsf{S}^{k}(\mathfrak{g})\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{Der}(A)\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
is an action of the
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
on the dg commutative algebra
(A,\cdot,d)
, we deduce that
\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,1}\}_{p=1}^{+\infty}
is an action of the action
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(A\otimes\mathfrak{g},\{l^{C}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
on the
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(A\otimes\mathfrak{g},\{\mathfrak{M}_{0,q}\}_{q=1}^{+\infty})
. Then, by Theorem
4.13
,
(A\otimes\mathfrak{g},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1})
is a post-Lie
∞
algebra.
∎

Recall from
[
13
,
30
,
45
,
53
]

that a
homotopy Poisson algebra
is a graded commutative algebra
(A,\cdot)
with an
L_{\infty}
-algebra structure
\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
, such that

\displaystyle l_{k}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k-1},x\cdot y)=l_{k}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k-1},x)\cdot y+(-1)^{x(1+x_{1}+\ldots+x_{k-1})}x\cdot l_{k}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k-1},y),

here
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k-1},x,y\in{A}.
We usually denote a homotopy Poisson algebra by
({A},\cdot,\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
.

Corollary 4.17
.

Let
({A},\cdot,\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
be a homotopy Poisson algebra. Then there is a post-Lie
∞
algebra structure on
A\otimes A
given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{0,1}(a_{1}\otimes x_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle l_{1}(a_{1})\otimes x_{1}+(-1)^{a_{1}}a_{1}\otimes l_{1}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{0,q}(a_{1}\otimes x_{1}\ldots a_{q}\otimes x_{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{q}(1+x_{1}+\ldots+x_{i-1})a_{i}}a_{1}\ldots a_{q}\otimes l_{q}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{q}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{p,1}\big((a_{1}\otimes x_{1}\ldots a_{p}\otimes x_{p})\otimes(a_{p+1}\otimes x_{p+1})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{p}(1+x_{1}+\ldots+x_{i-1})a_{i}}a_{1}\ldots a_{p}l_{p+1}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{p},a_{p+1})\otimes x_{p+1},

and

\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}=0
for other cases.

Proof.

Since
\{\mathsf{ad}_{k}:\mathsf{S}^{k}(A)\,\rightarrow\,\mathsf{Der}(A)\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
is an action of the
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(A,\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
on the dg commutative algebra
(A,\cdot,l_{1})
, by Theorem
4.16
, we deduce that
(A\otimes A,\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1})
is a post-Lie
∞
algebra.
∎

Corollary 4.18
.

Let
({A},\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\rho_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
be an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid. Then there is a post-Lie
∞
algebra structure on
A\otimes\mathfrak{g}
given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{0,q}(a_{1}\otimes x_{1}\ldots a_{q}\otimes x_{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{q}(1+x_{1}+\ldots+x_{i-1})a_{i}}a_{1}\ldots a_{q}\otimes l_{q}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{q}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{p,1}\big((a_{1}\otimes x_{1}\ldots a_{p}\otimes x_{p})\otimes(a_{p+1}\otimes x_{p+1})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{p}(1+x_{1}+\ldots+x_{i-1})a_{i}}a_{1}\ldots a_{p}\rho_{p}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{p})(a_{p+1})\otimes x_{p+1},

and

\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}=0
for other cases.

Proof.

Again, this is a straightforward consequence of Theorem
4.16
.
∎

To sum up, Theorem
4.16
, Corollary
4.17
and Corollary
4.18
demonstrate that there is a close relationship between post-Lie
∞
algebras and higher geometric structures.

5.
Homotopy Rota-Baxter operators and post-Lie
∞
algebras

In this section, we introduce the notion of homotopy Rota-Baxter operators on open-closed homotopy Lie algebras and establish their relationships with post-Lie
∞
algebras.

5.1.
Homotopy Rota-Baxter operators on open-closed homotopy Lie algebras

We first recall higher derived brackets, by which we construct an
L_{\infty}
-algebra from an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra. Then homotopy Rota-Baxter operators on an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra are defined to be Maurer-Cartan elements of this
L_{\infty}
-algebra.

Definition 5.1
.

(
[
53
]
)
A
V
-data consists of a quadruple
(L,H,P,\Phi)
where

\bullet

(L,[\cdot,\cdot])
is a graded Lie algebra,

\bullet

H
is an abelian graded Lie subalgebra of
(L,[\cdot,\cdot])
,

\bullet

P:L\,\rightarrow\,L
is a projection, that is
P\circ P=P
, whose image is
H
and kernel is a graded Lie subalgebra of
(L,[\cdot,\cdot])
,

\bullet

\Phi
is an element of
\ker(P)^{1}
such that
[\Phi,\Phi]=0
.

Theorem 5.2
.

(
[
53
]
)

Let
(L,H,P,\Phi)
be a
V
-data. Then
(H,\{{l_{k}}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra where

(52)

\displaystyle l_{k}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{k})=P[\ldots[[\Phi,a_{1}],a_{2}],\ldots,a_{k}],

for homogeneous elements
a_{1},\ldots,a_{k}\in H
. We call
\{{l_{k}}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
the
higher derived brackets
of the
V
-data
(L,H,P,\Phi)
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
be an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra. Any
f\in\mathrm{Hom}(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h}),\mathfrak{g})\subset\mathrm{Hom}(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h}),\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h})
gives rise to a coderivation on
\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h})
, which is denoted by
\hat{f}
.
Moreover, we denote by
\mathcal{H}
the graded vector space of coderivations on
\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h})
given by
\mathrm{Hom}(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h}),\mathfrak{g})
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
be an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra. Then the following quadruple is a V-data:

\bullet

the graded Lie algebra
(L,[\cdot,\cdot])
is given by
(\mathsf{coDer}(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h})),[\cdot,\cdot]_{C})
;

\bullet

the abelian graded Lie subalgebra
H
is given by
\mathcal{H};

\bullet

P:L\,\rightarrow\,L
is the projection onto the subspace
\mathcal{H}
;

\bullet

\Phi=\hat{\mathcal{R}},
where
\mathcal{R}=\sum_{k=1}^{+\infty}\mathcal{R}_{k}
is given by (
44
).

Consequently,
(\mathcal{H},\{\mathfrak{l}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra, where
\mathfrak{l}_{k}
is given by
(
52
).

Proof.

By
[
12
, Proposition 3.13 and Proposition 3.14]
,
\mathcal{H}
is an abelian graded Lie subalgebra and
\ker P
is a graded Lie subalgebra. Since
\mathcal{R}=\sum_{k=1}^{+\infty}\mathcal{R}_{k}
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra structure on
\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h}
, it follows that
[\Phi,\Phi]_{C}=0
. Moreover, (
44
), implies
P(\Phi)=0
. Thus
(L,H,P,\Phi)
is a V-data. Hence by Theorem
5.2
, we obtain higher derived brackets
\{{\mathfrak{l}_{k}}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
on
\mathcal{H}
.
∎

Definition 5.4
.

A degree
0
element
\Theta=\sum_{k=1}^{+\infty}\Theta_{k}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h}),\mathfrak{g})
is called a
homotopy Rota-Baxter operator
on an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
if it is a Maurer-Cartan element of the
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathcal{H},\{{\mathfrak{l}_{k}}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
; in other words the following identity holds:

(53)

\displaystyle P\big(e^{[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}}\Phi\big)=0.

Remark 5.5
.

Homotopy relative Rota-Baxter operators (also called
{\mathcal{O}}
-operators) on
L_{\infty}
-algebras with respect to
L_{\infty}
-actions of
L_{\infty}
-algebras were studied by Caseiro and Nunes da Costa in
[
12
]
. Since
L_{\infty}
-actions of
L_{\infty}
-algebras are special cases of open-closed homotopy Lie algebras, it follows that homotopy Rota-Baxter operators on open-closed homotopy Lie algebras contain homotopy relative Rota-Baxter operators as special cases.

Theorem 5.6
.

Let
\Theta=\sum_{k=1}^{+\infty}\Theta_{k}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h}),\mathfrak{g})
be a homotopy Rota-Baxter operator on an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
. Define
\alpha_{n}:\mathsf{S}^{n}(\mathfrak{h})\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{h}
by

(54)

\displaystyle\alpha_{n}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})=\mathfrak{R}_{0,n}(u_{1}\ldots u_{n})+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k+1}=n\atop i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\geq 1,i_{k+1}\geq 0}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k+1})}}\frac{\varepsilon(\sigma)}{k!}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{k,i_{k+1}}\Big(\Theta_{i_{1}}(u_{\sigma(1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(i_{1})})\ldots\Theta_{i_{k}}(u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k-1}+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k})})\otimes u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k}+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(n)}\Big).

Then
(\mathfrak{h},\{\alpha_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra and
\Theta
is a homomorphism from
(\mathfrak{h},\{\alpha_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
to
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
.

Proof.

Since
[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}
is a locally nilpotent derivation on the graded Lie algebra
(\mathsf{coDer}(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h})),[\cdot,\cdot]_{C})

[
32
]
, it follows that
e^{[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}}
is an automorphism of
(\mathsf{coDer}(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h})),[\cdot,\cdot]_{C})
. Therefore,
e^{[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}}\Phi
is a differential on the coalgebra
\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h})
, and gives rise to an
L_{\infty}
-algebra structure on
\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h}
.

Since
\Theta\in\mathrm{Hom}(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h}),\mathfrak{g})
, it follows that
\hat{\Theta}
is a locally nilpotent coderivation of the coalgebra
\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h})
, and
e^{\hat{\Theta}}
is a well-defined automorphism of the coalgebra
\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h})
. For all
u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\in\mathfrak{h},
we have

(55)

\displaystyle e^{\hat{\Theta}}(u_{1}\ldots u_{n})=u_{1}\ldots u_{n}+\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\sum_{i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k+1}=n\atop i_{1},\ldots,i_{k+1}\geq 1}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k+1})}}\frac{\varepsilon(\sigma)}{k!}

\displaystyle\Theta_{i_{1}}(u_{\sigma(1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(i_{1})})\ldots\Theta_{i_{k}}(u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k-1}+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k})})u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k}+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(n)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k}=n\atop i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\geq 1}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k})}}\frac{\varepsilon(\sigma)}{k!}\Theta_{i_{1}}(u_{\sigma(1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(i_{1})})\ldots\Theta_{i_{k}}(u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k-1}+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(n)}).

Moreover, since
[\Phi,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}=\Phi\circ\hat{\Theta}-\hat{\Theta}\circ\Phi
, and by induction, we have

(56)

\displaystyle\underbrace{[\ldots[[}_{k}\Phi,\hat{\Theta}]_{C},\hat{\Theta}]_{C},\ldots,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}=\sum_{i=0}^{k}(-1)^{i}{k\choose i}\underbrace{\hat{\Theta}\circ\ldots\circ\hat{\Theta}}_{i}\circ\Phi\circ\underbrace{\hat{\Theta}\circ\ldots\circ\hat{\Theta}}_{k-i}.

Therefore, we have

(57)

\displaystyle e^{[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}}\Phi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\underbrace{[\ldots[[}_{k}\Phi,\hat{\Theta}]_{C},\hat{\Theta}]_{C},\ldots,\hat{\Theta}]_{C},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\sum_{i=0}^{k}(-1)^{i}\frac{1}{i!(k-i)!}\underbrace{\hat{\Theta}\circ\ldots\circ\hat{\Theta}}_{i}\circ\Phi\circ\underbrace{\hat{\Theta}\circ\ldots\circ\hat{\Theta}}_{k-i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-\hat{\Theta}}\circ\Phi\circ e^{\hat{\Theta}}.

Equivalently, we have the following commutative diagram:

(58)

\textstyle{\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e^{[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}}\Phi}
\scriptstyle{e^{\hat{\Theta}}}
\textstyle{\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e^{\hat{\Theta}}}
\textstyle{\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h}),}

which implies that
e^{\hat{\Theta}}
is a homomorphism from the
L_{\infty}
-algebra
\big(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h}),\bar{\Delta},e^{[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}}\Phi\big)
to the
L_{\infty}
-algebra
\big(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h}),\bar{\Delta},\Phi\big)
.

By (
53
), we obtain that
\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h})
is an
L_{\infty}
-subalgebra of
\big(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h}),\bar{\Delta},e^{[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}}\Phi\big)
. More precisely, for all
u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\in\mathfrak{h},
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}_{\mathfrak{h}}\big(e^{[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}}\Phi\big)(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{twistor}}}{{=}}\mathrm{pr}_{\mathfrak{h}}\big(e^{-\hat{\Theta}}\circ\Phi\circ e^{\hat{\Theta}}\big)(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{iso-coalgebra}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k+1}=n\atop i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\geq 1,i_{k+1}\geq 0}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k+1})}}\frac{\varepsilon(\sigma)}{k!}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{k,i_{k+1}}\Big(\Theta_{i_{1}}(u_{\sigma(1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(i_{1})})\ldots\Theta_{i_{k}}(u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k-1}+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k})})\otimes u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k}+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(n)}\Big)

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{R}_{0,n}(u_{1}\ldots u_{n}),

which is exactly
\alpha_{n}
given by (
54
).

Since
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}\}_{p+q=1}^{+\infty})
is an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra, we obtain the short exact sequence of
L_{\infty}
-algebras with strict morphisms:

0\longrightarrow\big(\mathfrak{h},\{\mathfrak{R}_{0,q}\}_{q=1}^{+\infty}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\longrightarrow}}\big(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h},\{\mathcal{R}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\big(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}\big)\longrightarrow 0.

Here the linear maps
\iota
and
p
are given by

(59)

\displaystyle\iota(u)=(0,u),\,\,\,p(x,u)=x,\,\,\,\forall x\in\mathfrak{g},\,\,\,u\in\mathfrak{h}.

Since
\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h})
is an
L_{\infty}
-subalgebra of
\big(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h}),\bar{\Delta},e^{[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}}\Phi\big)
, it is clear that the embedding map

i:\big(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h}),\bar{\Delta},e^{[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}}\Phi|_{\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h})}\big)\,\rightarrow\,\big(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{h}),\bar{\Delta},e^{[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}}\Phi\big)

is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra homomorphism. By (
58
),
\bar{p}\circ e^{\hat{\Theta}}\circ i
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra homomorphism from
(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h}),\bar{\Delta},e^{[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}}\Phi|_{\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h})})
to
(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}),\bar{\Delta},\Psi(\sum_{k=1}^{+\infty}l_{k}))
. For all
u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\in\mathfrak{h},
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\big((\bar{p}\circ e^{\hat{\Theta}}\circ i)(u_{1}\ldots u_{n})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\big(\bar{p}(e^{\hat{\Theta}}(u_{1}\ldots u_{n}))\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{iso-coalgebra},\eqref{open-closed-to-extension}}}{{=}}\Theta_{n}(u_{1}\ldots u_{n}),

which implies that
\bar{\Theta}=\bar{p}\circ e^{\hat{\Theta}}\circ i
. Thus, we deduce that
\Theta
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra homomorphism from
(\mathfrak{h},\{\alpha_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
to
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
. The proof is finished.
∎

Remark 5.7
.

Theorem
5.6
above generalizes
[
12
, Proposition 3.10]
and
[
32
, Proposition 5.11]
.

We now give an explicit description of homotopy Rota-Baxter operators.

Proposition 5.8
.

\Theta=\sum_{k=1}^{+\infty}\Theta_{k}\in\mathrm{Hom}^{0}(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h}),\mathfrak{g})
is a homotopy Rota-Baxter operator on the open-closed homotopy Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
if and only if the following equalities hold for all
n\geq 1
and all homogeneous elements
u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\in\mathfrak{h}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k}=n\atop i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\geq 1}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k})}}\frac{\varepsilon(\sigma)}{k!}l_{k}\Big(\Theta_{i_{1}}(u_{\sigma(1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(i_{1})}),\ldots,\Theta_{i_{k}}(u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k-1}+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(n)})\Big)

(60)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(k,n-k)}}\varepsilon(\sigma)\Theta_{n-k+1}\Big(\mathfrak{R}_{0,k}(u_{\sigma(1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(k)})u_{\sigma(k+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(n)}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k+1}=n\atop i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\geq 1,i_{k+1}\geq 0}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k+1})}}\frac{\varepsilon(\sigma)}{k!}

\displaystyle\Theta_{i_{k+1}+1}\Big(\mathfrak{R}_{k,0}\big(\Theta_{i_{1}}(u_{\sigma(1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(i_{1})})\ldots\Theta_{i_{k}}(u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k-1}+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k})})\big)u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k}+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(n)}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\sum_{i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k+2}=n\atop i_{1},\ldots,i_{k+1}\geq 1,i_{k+2}\geq 0}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k+2})}}\frac{\varepsilon(\sigma)}{k!}

\displaystyle\Theta_{i_{k+2}+1}\Big(\mathfrak{R}_{k,i_{k+1}}\big(\Theta_{i_{1}}(u_{\sigma(1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(i_{1})})\ldots\Theta_{i_{k}}(u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k-1}+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k})})\otimes u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k}+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k+1})}\big)

\displaystyle u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k+1}+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(n)}\Big).

Proof.

A degree 0 element
\Theta=\sum_{k=1}^{+\infty}\Theta_{k}\in\mathrm{Hom}^{0}(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h}),\mathfrak{g})
is a homotopy Rota-Baxter operator on the open-closed homotopy Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}\}_{p+q=1}^{+\infty})
if and only if for all
n\geq 1
and all homogeneous elements
u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\in\mathfrak{h}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\big(e^{[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}}\Phi\big)(u_{1}\ldots u_{n})=0.

By (
57
), (
55
) and similar calculations as the proof of Theorem
5.6
, one can show that this is equivalent to (
60
).

∎

5.2.
Post-Lie
∞
algebras and homotopy Rota-Baxter operators

In this subsection, we study the relation between post-Lie
∞
algebras and homotopy Rota-Baxter operators on the open-closed homotopy Lie algebras given by
L_{\infty}
-actions of
L_{\infty}
-algebras.

Observe that a post-Lie
∞
algebra naturally gives rise to a homotopy Rota-Baxter operator.

Theorem 5.9
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1})
be a post-Lie
∞
algebra. Then the identity map
\rm{Id}:\mathfrak{g}\,\rightarrow\,\mathfrak{g}
is a homotopy Rota-Baxter operator on the open-closed homotopy Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g},\{l^{C}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.12
,
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g},\{l^{C}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
is an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra. By (
48
), we deduce that
\rm{Id}
is a homotopy Rota-Baxter operator on the open-closed homotopy Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g},\{l^{C}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p+q=1}^{+\infty})
.
∎

Now we show that homotopy Rota-Baxter operators on the open-closed homotopy Lie algebras given by
L_{\infty}
-actions of
L_{\infty}
-algebras induce post-Lie
∞
algebras. This generalizes
[
1
, Proposition 5.1]
,
[
6
, Theorem 5.4]
and
[
50
, Theorem 3.11]
.

Theorem 5.10
.

Let
\Theta=\sum_{k=1}^{+\infty}\Theta_{k}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h}),\mathfrak{g})
be a homotopy Rota-Baxter operator on an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra given by an
L_{\infty}
-action
\rho=\{\rho_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
of an
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
on an
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathfrak{h},\{\mu_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
. Then
(\mathfrak{h},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1})
is a post-Lie
∞
algebra, where
\mathfrak{M}_{0,q}=\mu_{q}
and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}(u_{1}\ldots u_{p}\otimes u_{p+1}\ldots u_{p+q})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{p}\sum_{i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k}=p\atop i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\geq 1}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k})}}\frac{\varepsilon(\sigma)}{k!}\mathfrak{R}_{k,q}\Big(\Theta_{i_{1}}(u_{\sigma(1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(i_{1})})\ldots\Theta_{i_{k}}(u_{\sigma(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{k-1}+1)}\ldots u_{\sigma(p)})\otimes u_{p+1}\ldots u_{p+q}\Big),

where the maps
\{\mathfrak{R}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 1,q\geq 1}
are given by (
4.1
).

Proof.

First we observe that for
\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1}
defined above,
\{l^{C}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{+\infty}
defined by (
48
) is exactly the
L_{\infty}
-algebra structure
(\mathfrak{h},\{\alpha_{n}\}_{n=1}^{+\infty})
given in Theorem
5.6
.

By Theorem
5.6
,
\bar{\Theta}
is a homomorphism from the dg coalgebra
(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h}),\bar{\Delta},e^{[\cdot,\hat{\Theta}]_{C}}\Phi|_{\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h})})
to the dg coalgebra
(\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{g}),\bar{\Delta},\Psi(\sum_{k=1}^{+\infty}l_{k}))
. Moreover, since
\rho
is an
L_{\infty}
-action of the
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\{l_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
on the
L_{\infty}
-algebra
(\mathfrak{h},\{\mu_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
, we deduce that
\bar{\rho}\circ\bar{\Theta}:\bar{\mathsf{S}}(\mathfrak{h})\,\rightarrow\,\bar{\mathsf{S}}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathsf{coDer}}(\mathsf{S}(\mathfrak{h})))
is a homomorphism of dg coalgebras. Thus,

\bar{\rho}\circ\bar{\Theta}
is an
L_{\infty}
-action of
(\mathfrak{h},\{\alpha_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
on
(\mathfrak{h},\{\mu_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty})
. Equivalently,
(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{h},\{l^{C}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{+\infty},\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p+q\geq 1})
is an open-closed homotopy Lie algebra such that
\mathfrak{M}_{p,0}=0
for all
p\geq 1
. By Theorem
4.13
, we deduce that
\{\mathfrak{M}_{p,q}\}_{p\geq 0,q\geq 1}
is a post-Lie
∞
algebra structure on
\mathfrak{h}
.
∎
